# THE DAWNING AURORA,

## O R

### THE ROOT OF PHILOSOPHY,
### ASTROLOGY, AND THEOLOGY;

Containing a description of nature, in which it is explained how everything was in the beginning; how nature and the elements became creaturely <note>The term "creaturely" refers to the state of being a created thing or being, as opposed to the uncreated nature of the Divine.</note>; what the two qualities, good and evil, are, from which everything takes its origin; how these two qualities exist and act now, and what they will be at the end of time; what the kingdom of God is and the infernal kingdom; and how men operate creaturely in both:

**THE** whole set forth with care, based upon a true foundation, in the knowledge of the spirit, and by divine impulse;

<meta>This page serves as a brief "Translator’s Note" by Louis-Claude de Saint-Martin, appearing immediately after the title page of the second volume. It establishes continuity with the first volume by referencing the linguistic choices made to accommodate Jakob Böhme’s unique mystical terminology.</meta>

# TRANSLATOR’S NOTE.

*The reader has seen in the first volume, at the end of the foreword, what should be understood by the words: inqualification, <note>original: "inqualification" — a term Böhme used to describe the inner-working or penetration of qualities within the divine nature.</note> body, or circumscription, begetting, etc.*

*Independantly of these various words, several others are found, either in this volume or in the preceding one, which are not standard French, such as to "originize" oneself, <note>original: "s’originiser" — a neologism created to express the act of something arising from its own source or origin.</note> enflaming, depreciator, etc.; but these are so clear that they will be easily understood.*

*The word "geniture" <note>original: "géniture" — an archaic term for offspring or the act of begetting.</note> has slipped by error into several places in this second volume. Although this word is French, it is not of a high enough register to hold its place in such serious matters. The reader is asked to substitute the word "begetting" or "generation" <note>original: "engendrement ou génération."</note> in its place.*

<image-desc>A circular institutional stamp at the bottom of the page, appearing to be a library mark with a crown in the center and text around the perimeter, though the specific text is partially obscured.</image-desc>

<vocab>inqualification, body, circumscription, begetting, to originize oneself, enflaming, depreciator, geniture, generation</vocab>

<summary>In this note, the translator Louis-Claude de Saint-Martin justifies his use of neologisms and specialized terms required to convey Böhme's mystical concepts, while offering a correction for a stylistic error regarding the word "geniture."</summary>
<keywords>translation theory, neologisms, Jakob Böhme, Louis-Claude de Saint-Martin, mysticism, French language, terminology</keywords>

# THE DAWN
## ARISING.

### CHAPTER FOURTEEN.

->How Lucifer, the most beautiful angel in heaven, became the most hideous demon?<-

->*The house of murders.*<-

1. Here, King Lucifer, cover your eyes, so as not to see your celestial crown being stripped away; you can no longer rule in heaven. Yet, wait just a little longer. First, we wish to gaze upon you, and see what a beautiful bride <note>original: "épouse" — Böhme often uses the metaphor of a bride to describe the soul or an angelic being in its state of union with the Divine.</note> you once were, and whether by some chance you might wash away the filth of your prostitution <note>In this context, "prostitution" refers to the spiritual betrayal of turning away from God toward the self.</note> so that you might become beautiful once again. We wish to briefly describe your chastity and your virtue.

2. Step forward, you philosophers and defenders of King Lucifer; the time has come, draw near and warn him while he still wears the crown; for we are about to hold a criminal trial concerning him; if you can justify him, then he shall be your king; if not, he must be cast down into

The author uses the metaphor of a fallen bride to describe Lucifer's lost purity and challenges his "defenders" to justify his actions before his inevitable expulsion.</summary>

->*Now observe.*<-

3. When Lucifer had so horribly corrupted himself, all his source-spirits <note>original: "sources-esprits" — in Böhme’s mystical system, these are the fundamental spiritual forces or "fountains" that generate a being's nature</note> became an enmity against God, for they all *qualified* <note>original: "qualifièrent" — a technical term in this context meaning to act, operate, or manifest a specific quality or power</note> quite differently than God, and there was an eternal enmity between God and Lucifer.

->*Here someone might ask:*<-

How long did Lucifer remain in the light of God?

->*The depth.*<-

4. When the royal kingdom <note>original: "circonscription royale" — this refers to the specific celestial territory or "circumference" assigned to a high-ranking angel</note> of Lucifer was embodied, in that same instant, light also ignited within Lucifer. For as soon as his source-spirits began to qualify for the structure of his kingdom, and to engender themselves, as was their natural right, then the flash of life rose in the heart, in the source of the sweet water <note>original: "l'eau suave" — one of the seven qualities of nature in Böhme's cosmology, representing the gentle, cooling, and receptive element</note>, and the royal kingdom was accomplished, and the spirit *which was* in the heart carried itself from the light through the mouth into the heart of God.
5. Then he was an exceedingly beautiful prince and king, very beloved and very pleasing to the divine being, and he was received with great joy. The spirit likewise moved from the heart into all the source-veins of the kingdom and ignited all the seven spirits <note>original: "sept esprits" — these are the seven primary qualities or "propertied" forces that Böhme believes constitute both God and the universe</note>; then the royal kingdom was immediately glorified, and he was like a divine king in a

inexpressible clarity, which surpassed that of all the celestial legions.

6. Now, in this brilliant and luminous flash, the seven source-spirits <note>original: "sources-esprits" — the seven fundamental forces or "qualities" in Böhme's cosmology that make up all of nature and spirit</note> were immediately ignited, as when a fire is lit. For they were surprised by the marvelous clarity of their spirit, and at the first glimmer, or at the first sight, they instantly became highly triumphant, glorious, magnificent, and extremely joyful, and were stirred toward a higher generation <note>original: "génération" — here referring to the process by which spiritual forces give birth to or manifest new states of being</note>.

7. But if they had remained in their seat, and had qualified <note>original: "qualifié" — the technical term for how these spiritual forces operate and interact with one another</note> as they had done throughout all eternity, then this high light would not have harmed them. For they were not new spirits formed from something foreign, but they were the ancient spirits who had no beginning, who had been eternally in God, who knew well the rights of God and of nature, and how they ought to conduct themselves.

8. Also, when God configured the *body*, He did not beforehand destroy the source-spirits, but He formed the *body* of King Lucifer from the core of what was best in them, and in which the most perfect knowledge resided.

9. Otherwise, if the qualities had been destroyed beforehand, they would have then needed a new life; they would have been in doubt as to whether an angel could exist eternally.

->*Understand this precisely.*<-

10. God created the angels from himself, so that their conglomeration <term>conglomeration: the gathering or binding together of spiritual forces into a stable, individual form</term> would have more endurance and consistency than do the ideas and figures that arise in nature through the *qualifying* <term>qualifying: the active operation or manifestation of the innate powers (qualities) of a spirit</term> of the spirits of God, and which are destroyed in their turn by the movement of the spirits; and so that through their *concretion* <note>original: "concrétion" — the solidification of spiritual essence into a more tangible, lasting state</note> the light might shine with greater clarity, and that the tone of the *body* might ring and resonate in a clearer manner; by this means the kingdom of joy was to increase in God, and such is the reason why God created the angels.

11. But as for what has been said, that the angel had fathered a new light, or a new spirit; here is how it must be understood.

12. As the source-spirits <note>original: "sources-esprits" — Böhme's term for the seven fundamental forces of nature and divinity</note> had been more strongly conglomerated, the light then appeared much clearer within the *body* and outside the *body* than previously in the *salliter* <note>original: "salliter" — Böhme’s spelling of "salniter" (saltpeter), representing the volatile, combustible divine "soil" or essential material of the spiritual world</note>; for there arose within the *body* a flash more resplendent than before, since the *salliter* was more active.

13. Now, it is for this reason that the source-spirits became presumptuous, and persuaded themselves that they had a more brilliant light or a more beautiful son than was the Son of God; this is also why they wished to in-qualify <note>original: "inqualifier" — to intensify their internal spiritual activity or to work within their own power rather than God's</note> and elevate themselves more strongly; they despised the *in-qualification* which was in God their father, as well as the generation of the Son of God and the procession of God the Holy Spirit, and thought they cou-

14. They knew well that they were not the Universal God, but they were a [spark] or a portion of Him. They also knew well to what extent His omnipotence reached; nevertheless, they no longer wanted what was ancient <note>i.e., the original, humble state of their creation</note>, but they wanted to be more elevated than the Universal God; and they persuaded themselves that they should extend their empire over the universal Divinity and over all the kingdoms.

15. This is why they exalted themselves with the intention of carrying their fire into the entire divinity, and of governing the Universal God by their power; their goal was to produce within their *inqualification* <term>inqualification: Böhme's term for the inner working or "qualifying" of the spiritual forces within a being</term> all forms and all images, to dominate God, and to suffer no rival.

16. This, then, is the source of jealousy, envy, pride, and anger; for in this fiery *inqualification*, anger rose up and burned like a hot and cold fire, and even as bitter as gall.

17. For the source-spirits <note>original: "sources-esprits" — the seven fundamental forces of the divine nature</note> had no foreign impulse within them; rather, the impulse toward pride arose within the body, within the wheel <note>original: "roue" — referring to the "Wheel of Nature," the cyclical and self-generating interaction of the seven spirits</note> of the seven source-spirits, which joined together with the intent of being the only God.

18. But as in their ancient seat they could neither begin nor put into effect

...in this enterprise, they <note>referring to the internal forces or source-spirits of the rebellious angels</note> deceived one another to the point of wanting to rise up against the generation of God; they wished to *inqualify* <term>inqualify: Böhme's term for the way spiritual forces interact, vibrate, and manifest their specific characteristics within a being</term> even into the greatest depth. At that point, nothing would have been equal to them, since they were the most powerful prince within God.

19. The **astringent quality** <note>original: "la qualité astringente" — the contracting, hardening force that seeks to bind and solidify</note> was the first murderer and the first seducer; for when it saw that it had thus generated a beautiful light, it contracted even more forcefully than God had constrained it, intending to become even more terrifying, to attract and concentrate everything within the universality of its region, and to hold it there as a powerful sovereign. In this way, it produced the substance from which the earth and stones take their origin, which I shall describe when I treat the creation of the world.

20. The **bitter quality** was the second murderer. When it rose up like a flash of lightning, it became tearing and shattering within the astringent quality with great power, as if it wished to tear the body to pieces. The astringent quality did not oppose this; it was not because it could not have taken the bitter spirit prisoner and bathed it in the **sweet water** <note>original: "eau suave" — the mild, cooling, and harmonious element of the divine nature</note> until its pride was dissipated. Rather, it desired to have such a brother, for this suited its purpose; otherwise, since the bitter spirit also takes its origin from it—the astringent being its father—it could have restrained it.

21. **Heat** was the third spirit of murder; it had killed its mother, the **sweet water**; but the spirit

the **astringent quality** is the cause, for by its powerful contraction and its great compression, it has thus strongly awakened and inflamed the fire by means of the **bitter quality**; now, the fire is the sword of the astringent and bitter qualities.

22. But as the fire rises within the **sweet water** <note>original: "eau suave" — in Böhme's thought, this represents the mild, light-filled substance of the divine realm</note>, it possesses within its own power the instrument of correction, and it could have restrained the astringent quality within the water; but it [ *the heat* ] was also a seductress. It practiced dissimulation <note>the act of concealing one's true motives or nature</note> toward the highest quality—namely, the astringent—and it aided in killing the sweet water.

23. The **tone** <term>tone: "le ton," referring to the spiritual sound or resonance that gives form and expression to the inner qualities</term> is the fourth murderer, for it takes its resonance from the fire, *which is in the sweet water*, and it rises gracefully throughout the body.

24. It did not follow this law; but after it had risen into the water and into the astringent quality, it also conspired with the astringent quality, and it burst forth as violently as a clap of thunder, wishing thereby to announce its own divinity; and the fire rose up, as when it produces lightning, by which means they persuaded themselves that they were great in God, above all things.

25. And they conducted themselves thus until they had killed their mother, the sweet water; then the universality <note>the total collective existence</note> of the body was nothing but darkness, and there was no longer any remedy in God that could have been of use here. Love was transmuted into enmity; the entire body had become a black and dark **Demon**.

26. The word **TEU** [ *the first syllable of the word for demon* ] <note>Böhme is likely referring to the German word "Teufel" (Devil). He often used "sensual language" to break words into syllables to reveal their spiritual essence. Though the French translator uses "Démon," the syllable "TEU" relates to the German root for "Devil."</note>

### *The wondrous manifestation.*

28. See; King Lucifer was the leader in his entire region, and he was a powerful king; he was produced from the heart of his entire region, and through his uprising, he wished to inflame his entire region, so that all could burn and <term>operate within</term> <note>original: "inqualifier" — a technical term in Böhme's theosophy describing how spiritual qualities or forces interact, blend, and manifest their nature within a being</note> just as he did in his own body.

31. his water <gloss>* (This water is the water of eternal life,
engendered in the light of majesty; but
in the center, it is compared to a spirit of
sulfur, or else to aqua fortis <note>original: "eau forte"—literally "strong water," the alchemical term for nitric acid, a corrosive substance. Böhme uses it here to describe how the life-giving "water" of heaven became a burning, acidic quality in the fallen angels.</note>.)</gloss> dried up completely and ignited, and
his light went out; then all was consumed.

## On the fall of all his angels.

->*Now someone might say:*<-

->How did it happen then that all his angels par-<-
->took in his fall?<-

33. According to what the master commanded, his subjects
obeyed. When he raised himself up and wished to be God,
his angels, seeing this, all did as he did,
and all acted as if they wished to launch an assault
against the divinity. For they were all subject to him,
and he reigned in all his angels, given that he had
been created from the core of the <term>salitter</term> <note>A term unique to Böhme, derived from "saltpeter." It refers to the divine, crystalline substance or "earth" of the heavenly kingdom from which spiritual beings are formed.</note>, from which all his
angels had been created at once, and he was the
chief of all his angels.

34. That is why they all acted as he did,
and all wished to establish themselves in supremacy,
above the divinity, and to rule sovereignly
with their chief, above all the divine power;
there was in them all but one single will,
and they did not allow it to be taken from them.

->Now you will say:<-

Did the universal God not know then, before—

manifested. Otherwise, if evil were manifested there, love would not be gentleness itself, and humility. But in the pronouncing of his word; there where the nature of the spiritual world <insert>originates itself;</insert> where one conceives perceptibility; where God names himself a jealous, angry God, and a consuming fire; it is there that he has well known from all eternity that if he were ever to move within this source, it would also become creaturely; however, there he is not called God, but a consuming fire.

It is magically <note>In Böhme’s theology, "magical" refers to the spiritual process by which the divine Will or Desire moves into manifestation or "nature."</note> that in this writing, I understand how the love and the wrath of God are different, and how the <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "science." In this context, it refers to an experiential awareness or a specific "mode" of being.</note> of evil, or of the demon and the fall, was known only from its source, from which the fall also took its origin. It is also only in love that the source and the knowledge of the kingdom of joy is found within God; for every knowledge produces that which is like itself.

Now if I were to say: that in God love would have willed evil, or that in God there would be a false knowledge within love and gentleness, I would speak against scripture; for what love in God knows as perceptible within itself is sufficient for it, and it wants nothing more; and what wrath in God knows as perceptible within itself is all the knowledge that it has, and it knows nothing else. It is from this that evil and good exist in creation, and I warn the reader to rightly understand our profound meaning, and not to go astray here, but to read our other writings, where this is sufficiently clarified. )

### *Of the great transgressions, the opposing will, and the eternal enmity of King Lucifer and all his legions against God.*

37. This is the true mirror of man; the spirit summons all men before this tribunal of evildoers, as if before a mirror where they may contemplate themselves and see what hidden <term>sin</term> <note>In Böhme’s view, sin is not just a moral failing but a structural "turning away" of the will from the light of God toward the self-centered "fire" of the soul.</note> is.

38. This has remained unknown since the beginning of the world, and has never been so entirely revealed to the heart of any man. I myself marvel at this profound manifestation, perhaps even more than the reader will marvel at it.

39. I do not write this for my own glory; for my glory consists in my hope for the future. I am also a poor sinner like all men, and this mirror awaits me as it does them. But I marvel that God should wish to manifest himself so entirely to such a simple man <note>The author, Jacob Böhme, was a cobbler by trade and frequently reminded his readers that his insights came from divine inspiration rather than university education.</note>, and that, furthermore, he still urges him to write such things, whereas there would be many better pens that could describe and set them forth much more perfectly than I, who am but the plaything and the object of contempt of this world.

40. But I cannot, nor do I wish to, resist him: for I have often been in great <term>struggle</term> <note>original: "travail." This word suggests both hard labor and the internal "travail" or agony of a soul wrestling with spiritual impulses.</note> against him, for fear that I was not being moved by him, and that he would not accept such things from me; but I have recognized that through my struggle against him, I have done nothing but gather materials for this edifice.

<summary>The author reflects on the mirror of the soul which reveals hidden sin and expresses his personal humility and surprise at being chosen by God to reveal these profound spiritual truths despite his low social standing.</summary>

41. Now, I have raised myself so high that I no longer dare to look back, for fear that dizziness might seize me; and I have only a small portion of the ladder <note>Böhme frequently uses the "Jacob’s Ladder" as a metaphor for the stages of spiritual illumination. For him, looking back represents returning to a worldly or purely rational mindset, which causes spiritual "vertigo."</note> left to climb to reach entirely the end toward which all the desires of my heart tend: for when I climb, I feel no dizziness at all; but when I look back and wish to return, my head becomes troubled and I fear falling.

42. That is why I have placed my trust in the powerful God, and I wish to attempt and see what will happen to me. Besides, I have only a body which, without this, would be no less mortal and corruptible; all the more reason for me to make this attempt. Provided only that the light and the knowledge of my God remain with me, I shall then have all that is necessary for this life and for the next.

43. Also, I shall not complain at all, even if I should by chance have to suffer contempt because of his name, which flourishes for me more and more, and into which I am, as it were, naturalized. I will say with the prophet David: even if my body and my soul should succumb to it, you would nonetheless be, O my God! my security, my salvation, and the consolation of my heart (Ps. 73:26).

44. Sin has seven kinds or seven forms, among which there are four principal bubbling-sources <note>original: "sources-bouillonnantes." In Böhme's German, this relates to *Quellgeister* (fountain-spirits). The word implies a "boiling" or "agonizing" energy that is the source of all manifestation, whether in light or in darkness.</note>, and the eighth form is the house of death.

->*Now observe.*<-

45. The seven forms are the seven <term>source-spirits</term> <note>The "source-spirits" (German: *Quellgeister*) are the seven core qualities or properties of nature in Böhme's system—such as the "astringent," the "bitter," and the "sweet"—which interact to create all reality.</note>

49. Come then here, you philosophers and doctors, who wish to defend yourselves and who strive to maintain that God created evil, and that he wills it; that it is by God's design that the demon fell, and that a great number of men are lost, and that he could have disposed everything otherwise.

50. Here the spirit of our kingdom, as well as your prince Lucifer, whom you defend, summon you for the third time before the final tribunal of evildoers. Give your answer there: for, regarding these seven species and the four new sons, the proceedings will be rigorously followed in the house of the heavenly father.

51. If you can maintain that the seven spirits of Lucifer have begotten with justice and equity the four new sons <note>These "sons" were identified on the previous page as pride, greed, envy, and wrath—the corruptions of the original divine qualities.</note>, so that, according to equity and justice, they would have the right to govern heaven and the universal divinity; then King Lucifer must once again be restored to his seat, and his kingdom must be returned to him.

52. Otherwise, a cavern or a dungeon must be prepared for him, to serve as an eternal prison, where he will be locked away forever with his sons; and you shall see if the court of justice will not also take an interest in you.

53. Since you wish, then, to plead the demon’s cause, with what will he pay your fees? He has nothing in his power but infernal abomination; what reward can you then obtain? Imagine, friends, what is best among his fruits and among the balms of his garden.

### *Of the first species.*

54. The first spirit is the astringent quality <note>original: "qualité astringente." In the author's cosmology, this is the first of the seven "source-spirits." It represents the power of contraction, cold, and "drawing in" to create form. In its pure state, it is the foundation of structure; in its corrupted state, it becomes harsh and rigid.</note> which, in God, concentrates, coagulates, and gently cools, and is employed in formation; and if in its depth there is something sharp, it is nonetheless tempered with the sweet water <note>original: "l'eau suave." This refers to a divine, fluid essence that softens the harshness of the primary qualities, representing mercy or grace.</note>, so that it is altogether gentle, lovely, and joyful.

55. And if the light of the sweet water enters into it, it joyfully and freely operates its generation there, and renders it dry and clear; and when the tone <note>original: "ton." The author frequently uses musical or harmonic metaphors to describe how the divine qualities resonate and manifest their character.</note> rises in the light, it joins there gently and—

...fraternally its resonance; thus she <note>Boehme refers to the "qualities" or "source-spirits" in the feminine, following the French gender for *qualité*. 56. He <note>The focus shifts here to the generative aspect of this divine essence.</note> is truly a humble father to his children; he cherishes them cordially, and he plays joyfully with them; for he is truly the father of the six other spirits, who are engendered within him, and he helps them all to engender.

57. Now when God created Lucifer with his legion, he created him from himself, out of this joyful divinity, from the place of heaven and of this world; there was, for this purpose, no other matter. This living <term>salitter</term> <note>Boehme's term for the "divine sap" or the fundamental substance of the universe, derived from the Latin *sal nitrum* (saltpeter), representing the explosive and nurturing potential of God's essence.</note> was contracted quite gently, without destruction, and without great agitation.

58. But the spirits corporized <note>original: "corporisé." This refers to the process of being given a distinct, spiritual body or form.</note> together possessed knowledge, science, and the eternal divine law which has no beginning. They knew well how the divinity was engendered. They also knew well that the heart of God held primacy in the universal divinity; they likewise knew that they had no other property, which they might use or dispose of, other than their own corporized circumscription <note>original: "circonscription." This refers to the boundary or "container" of their individual being, as opposed to the infinite nature of God.</note>; for they saw clearly that the divinity was engendered apart from their own boundaries, just as it had done from all eternity.

59. They also knew well that they were not the entirety of space, nor the universal place; rather, they were creatures within this same space, or within this same place. They knew they were meant to increase its joy and its marvelous proportions; they were meant to harmonize and <term>inter-qualify</term> <note>original: "inqualifier." This is a central concept in Jacob Boehme’s theosophy, describing how different spiritual qualities or forces penetrate and influence one another to create a unified experience.</note> with this same space or place of divinity, and to imbue themselves deliciously with the qualities that existed outside of their own bodies.

60. They also possessed the power to dispose as they wished of all configurations, forms, and vegetations; everything was a cordial play of love in God. They would not have excited any displeasure in God, their creator, even if they had broken all the configurations and all the celestial vegetations, or if they had sacrificed them to their pure amusement; God would always have caused enough others to grow for them, seeing as all of that was nothing but a play within God.

61. For the purpose of their creation had also been that they might play with these configurations and vegetations, and use them according to their will. Indeed, these configurations had manifested themselves in this way from all eternity, and were continually transmuted and varied by the <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "sources-esprits." These are the seven fundamental divine powers (such as the Sour, the Sweet, the Bitter, etc.) that Boehme describes as the "fountain-springs" of all reality.</note>; for such was the eternal play of God, before the time of the creation of the angels.

62. You have a beautiful example of this in the animals, the birds, and all the vegetations of this world, if you are willing to look at them and are not blind: for all these things were produced and grew before man was created—he who is, and signifies, the second legion that <note>The text breaks off here; Boehme often refers to the different "legions" of angels or spiritual beings, with Lucifer's being the first and the human spirit/Michael's being associated with subsequent "legions" or orders of creation.</note>

63. When God had thus gently <term>embodied</term> <note>original: "corporisée." 64. But as it could not achieve this alone, it employed guile toward the other spirits, so that they followed it as their father, and they did as it did, each within its own quality.

65. When they were thus reunited, they also generated a similar spirit, which burst forth through the mouth, through the eyes, through the ears, and through the nose, and mixed with the *salitter*, which was outside of their boundaries.

66. For here was the design of the astringent quality: finding itself so gloriously embodied, like the kernel of the totality of the kingdom, it also wished—by means of its spirit which it generated with the other spirits—to reign forcefully and sovereignly outside of its boundaries, in the universal *salitter* of God, in order to submit everything to its power.

67. It wanted, by means of the spirit it generated, to configure everything, and to form everything like

70. 74. You philosophers, and defenders of prince Lucifer, first justify the **astringent spirit** <note>The "astringent spirit" refers to the contracting, hardening force in Jacob Boehme’s mystical physics. In its balanced state, it provides structure; in Lucifer, it became a source of cold, hard egoism.</note> within Lucifer, and say whether he behaved well or not; now, you must demonstrate this to me in nature; I do not wish to receive as proof your twisted, forced writings, lacking in solidity, but I require living testimonies.

75. It is also living testimonies that I present to you; such as, in particular, the created and *tangible* heaven, the stars, the elements, the creatures, the earth, the stones, men, and finally your Lucifer himself, that dark prince—cold, hot, hard, harsh and wicked—by whose uprising all these things have become what they are.

76. Direct your defense of this spirit toward those facts; otherwise he shall be condemned. Yes, such are the rights of the God who has no beginning: that the child who is engendered of the mother should be humble before her, and that he should obey her, for it is from her who engendered him that he holds his life and his body.

77. Also, as long as the mother is alive, the house of the mother is not the property of the child. But it is through love that she keeps him near her, that she nourishes him, that she covers him with the most beautiful finery she has, and that she gives them to him as property, so that her joy may increase in her child, and that she may rejoice in him.

78. But if the child rebels against the mother,

->*Now, what did the astringent quality do with its mother, the sweet water?*<-

82. It flattered the bitter and hot qualities, and persuaded them that they should exalt and inflame themselves. Everything had to bow and bend before them, and they wanted to configure and form everything with their harshness.

83. Following this wicked resolution, they became as one, and they dried up the sweet water within the embodiment of Lucifer; the heat inflamed the water, and the astringent quality made it dry; then it became entirely sour and stinging.

84. Since it was through such qualities that they engendered the spirit of Lucifer, the life of the spirit that rose in the water—as well as the light—became acrid and stinging.

85. And if it pleases the spirit, it eats and imbues itself with that thing; it struggles with it, and tempers it. Whatever the reach of that thing might be, even to the point where it reaches the origin and principle of its reign in God, the spirit can in that instant govern that far, and it is held back by nothing.

91. 99. Yes, dear man; the wrath of God is in everything that is *perceivable* <note>original: "appercevable." In this context, it refers to the quality of being tangible or manifest. Böhme teaches that the "harshness" of material reality is a manifestation of God's "wrath" or "fire" principle, which provides the necessary resistance for form to exist.</note>, in money, gold, stones, fields, garments, animals, and men; without it, nothing would be so harshly *perceivable*.

100. But you must know that the kernel of love also exists in everything, in the hidden center, unless that center is entirely evil, which then does not please man.

(God possesses everything. Only, according to nature, He is not the essence; He possesses Himself.) <note>This distinction suggests that while God is present in all things, His true, divine essence remains distinct and self-contained, rather than being identical to the material objects themselves.</note> Now, do you imagine you do well to bathe yourselves in the wrath of God? Take care lest it inflame your body and soul, and that you burn eternally within it, like Lucifer.

101. But when God, at the end of this time, brings to light what is hidden, you will then clearly see where the love of God or His wrath has been. Therefore, pay attention and observe yourselves; turn your eyes away from evil, or else you will lose yourselves.

102. I take heaven and earth to witness that I have fulfilled here what God has revealed to me as His will.

103. Thus the king Lucifer changed the sweet water within his body into a stinging bitterness <term>original: *aigreur piquante*. In Böhme's cosmology, "sweet water" represents the harmonious, fluid state of divine light, while "bitterness" or "sting" represents the harsh, contracted state of the "wrath" of nature when separated from love.</term>, with the intention of ruling thereby over the universal divinity through his pride. 104. 105. This is why we rightly sing:
> In the midst of life, we are surrounded by death; where can we flee to reach grace? To You alone, Lord Christ. <note>original: "nous sommes au milieu de la vie..." 106. Now the hero is there in combat; we must fly toward him. He who is our king, Jesus Christ, possesses within himself the love of the Father; and he fights with divine strength and power against the burning hellish horrors.

107. It is toward him that we must flee; and it is he who preserves divine love in all things in this world, without which everything would be lost.

# CH. 14.

108. You, philosophers and reasoners <note>Böhme uses "reasoners" (French: *raisonneurs*) as a pejorative term for those who rely solely on human logic and intellectual speculation rather than spiritual revelation or "living" knowledge.</note>, who make God into a demon by saying that He wills evil, bring your answer here once again to see if you can defend your cause; otherwise, the harsh spirit <term>original: *esprit âpre*. In Böhme's cosmology, this is the *Grimmigkeit*—the fierce, astringent, or "wrathful" quality of nature. While it is a necessary part of God's power, it only becomes "evil" when it is separated from the light of His love, as happened in Lucifer.</term> within Lucifer must also be condemned as a corrupter and an enemy of God, as well as of all His heavenly legions.

<summary>In this passage, Böhme challenges intellectualists who claim God is the author of evil, arguing that such a view makes God no better than a demon. He asserts that if evil were God's will, then Lucifer's fallen nature could not be rightly condemned as an enemy of the divine.</summary>

# CHAPTER FIFTEEN.

### Concerning the third species, or the form of the beginning of sin in Lucifer.

1. The third spirit in God is the bitter spirit <term>bitter spirit: One of Böhme's seven "source-spirits." It represents mobility, the "sting" of life, and the power of differentiation. In its balanced state, it is the joy of movement; in its unbalanced state, it becomes bitterness or rage.</term> which exists in the flash of life <note>original: *l'éclair de la vie*. This corresponds to Böhme’s *Blitz*, the sudden ignition of consciousness and light within the divine forces.</note>, for the flash of life rises in the sweet water through the friction of the astringent and hot qualities; but the *body* of the flash remains in the sweet water in a peaceful manner like a light or a heart; now the flash is in a great trembling; and through the terror, the fire, the water, and the astringent spirit <term>astringent spirit: Also known as the "harsh" or "contracting" quality (*die Herbe* in German). It provides the structure or "salitter" that holds the other spirits together.</term>, it becomes bitter by passing through the radical source of the water, into which it rises.

2. This same flash, or this furious terror, or this bitter spirit, is imprisoned in the astringent quality; it is glorified in the clear light within the astringent spirit, and it is extremely joyful; now, this is the mobility or the root of life, which in the astringent quality shapes and subdivides the word, so that in the *body* there exists a thought or a will.

3. Then this same triumphant and extremely joyful spirit is admirably and excellently employed in the configurations within the divine *salitter* <term>salitter: A word Böhme coined to describe the divine "matter" or the crystalline, spiritual substance of the heavenly kingdom. It is the "divine earth" from which all heavenly forms are generated.</term>; for it boils primarily in tone and in love; and it is that which is closest to the

<summary>Böhme describes the "bitter spirit" as the source of mobility and will within the divine nature, explaining how it arises from the friction of elemental qualities like heat and astringency to create the "flash of life." This metaphysical chemistry explains the internal state of Lucifer before his pride led to his fall.</summary>

From this, Christ said: the devil is a liar and a murderer from the beginning, and he did not persevere in the truth. (John 8:44.)

9. But Lucifer imagined that he would, by this means, be above God; that no one would reign and govern in such an imposing manner as he; that everything must bow before him. He wished, by his spirit, to reign with power in the entire divinity, like a king who is above all; as he was the most beautiful, he also wished to be the most powerful.

10. He saw and knew well the humble and gentle being in God his father; furthermore, he knew well that the divinity had been from all eternity in this delicious state, and that he also should have begotten in a similar divine sweetness, like a tender and obedient son.

->II<-

<summary>This page describes the transformation of Lucifer's inner spirit into a wrathful, "infernal" state as his prideful will sought to dominate the Godhead, contrasting his arrogance with the eternal humility of God.</summary>

<header>CH. 15. ( 35 )</header>

11. But as he was then shaped in his beauty and in his power, like a king in nature, his beautiful form preoccupied him, and he said to himself: "I am now God and formed of God; who will defeat me, or who will change me? I myself shall be sovereign, and I will reign in everything through my sharp quality <note>original: *qualité aiguë*. This refers to one of the fundamental "qualities" or forces in Böhme’s cosmology, usually associated with piercing intensity or bitterness.</note>, and my body is an image that must be adored; I want to prepare a new kingdom for myself, for this whole region belongs to me; I am the only God, and there is none other."

12. And in his pride, he covered himself with blindness and darkness, and made himself a demon; this is also what he is and what he must remain eternally.

(He recognized in God only majesty, and not the word in the center, which is the principal organ; he blinded himself with the dark astringency <note>original: *astringence*. In Böhme's system, this is the first quality of nature: a contracting, hardening force that, when separated from light, creates darkness.</note>, for he wanted to ignite himself, and dominate in fire, over light and over sweetness).

13. When, therefore, this wicked and devilish spirit (meaning the center of the generator <term>generator: From the French *génératrice*. In Böhme's theosophy, this is the "Eternal Nature" or the "Mother" force that is constantly giving birth to the divine qualities.</term>) boiled and speculated <note>original: *spécula*. As noted previously, this implies an active, imaginative movement of the will to grasp or change reality.</note> in the divine *salitter* <term>salitter: A technical term used by Böhme to describe the fundamental constituent matter of the divine realm, similar to "spiritual earth."</term>, he did nothing but sting, burn, kill, and crush, and became a pure contradictor. For the heart of God desired love and sweetness; and Lucifer wanted, through violence, to drag this heart into tyranny.

14. Then there was nothing but enmity and opposition; he violently ignited the divine *salitter*, which, from all eternity, had rested in its sweetness.

15. It is from this ignition in this region that God now calls Himself a wrathful <note>original: *colé-*. Truncated from *colérique* (wrathful/angry), as the word continues on the next page.</note> God—

This rebellion corrupts the "salitter" (divine substance) of his region, transforming the experience of God from one of sweetness to one of wrath.</summary>

Everything that is in you, you throw into God. <note>In Böhme’s theology, the human soul is deeply interconnected with God's being; therefore, human emotions and intentions literally "infuse" or affect the divine qualities within the individual's experience.</note> (That is to say, into the generator of nature <term>generator of nature: the "Mother" or foundational force of Eternal Nature that constantly brings forth divine qualities.</term>; this is why it must be tested by fire, just like the spirit of the soul; there, wickedness must remain in the fire).

### How can this be?

16. When you open your bold eyes and turn them toward the being of God, it is then as if you were thrusting sharp spikes into the being of God, and you set the anger of God into motion. When the tone [*or the noise*] resounds in your ears until it is cut off from the being of God; then it is toward God as if you were making a thunderclap burst within him.

17. Take care regarding what you do with the organ of respiration, and with that of speech; through your discourse, your dear newborn son <note>Likely referring to the "Word" or the spirit generated by human thought and breath, which Böhme views as a miniature version of the divine birth.</note> leaps out, like a son of all the seven spirits; and see if you do not torment the salitter <term>salitter: A term coined by Böhme (possibly from "saltpeter") to describe the divine "sap" or the fundamental glorious substance of the heavenly world.</term> of God, just as Lucifer did. Alas! there is not the slightest difference.

18. On the other hand, God says: I am a merciful God to those who cherish me, and I [them...]

19. Such are those who, by opposing the flaming fires of wrath through their love, their gentleness, the continual ardor of their zeal, and through their prayers, extinguish the choleric fire <term>choleric fire: In the author's mystical system, this refers to the 'Grimmigkeit' or the harsh, consuming quality of God’s initial power when it is not tempered by Light and Love.</term> and resist the anger ignited by the enemy.

20. There are truly harsh blows there. For the blazing fire of God’s wrath often hurls itself upon them, so that they know not where to find rest; heavy mountains weigh upon them; the love of the cross <note>This refers to the spiritual embrace of suffering and sacrifice as a path to divine union, a common theme in 17th-century Christian mysticism.</note> presses them and makes its stings felt.

21. Now, this is their comfort and their powerful support against the fury and the ignited fire, as King David says: for the pious man, light dawns in the darkness. (Psalm 112:4).

22. Likewise, in this combat against the wrath of God, the flaming fury of the demon, and all impious men, the light dawns in the heart of the pious man; and the gracious love of God inflames him, so that he does not despair in his crosses <note>Here, "crosses" refers to personal trials, tribulations, or burdens.</note>, and fights all the more strongly against wrath and fury.

23. If there were not still, at all times, some pious men on earth who, by their resistance, extinguished the wrath of God, the infernal fire would have been ignited long ago; then you would see clearly where hell is, in which you do not believe at present.

27. Now, where there is water, there is no fire, but a gracious warmth, and a sweet qualification; but if

28. His intention was not to extinguish the light, but for it to burn within the fire; he proposed to dry up the water, imagining that the light moved within the burning fire. 38. The seven source-spirits derive their *body* or their density from nature—that is, from the seven spirits of *nature* within the *divine* power. 39. For it has a subtlety similar to that of the Holy Spirit of God; and it *in-qualifies* <note>original: "inqualifie" — a translation of Böhme’s technical term 'mitqualificiren,' meaning to share the same qualities, powers, or operations as another being</note> or works together with God, the Holy Spirit.

### *Of the fifth kind, or form of the beginning of sin in Lucifer and his angels.*

44. The fifth source-spirit <note>original: "source-esprit" — Böhme's term for the "Quellgeister" or the seven fundamental forces that constitute the divine nature and the soul.</note> in the divine power is gracious love, which is the true glance of gentleness and humility, and which is also generated in the flash of life. When the flash penetrates rapidly like a terror, from which joy results, then the mass of light lit in the sweet water holds fast and gently pursues the flash through the fire, even into the astringent quality <note>original: "qualité astringente" — the first property of nature, representing contraction, coldness, and hardness.</note>; it tempers the fire, and makes supple and soft the astringent quality, which is also a generation of the water.

45. Now, when the fire feels this delicate, sweet, and tender taste, it calms itself and transforms into a sweet, very pleasant warmth; and a lovely life rises in the fire; through this same lovely and sweet heat, the astringent quality penetrates and calms the cold fire, and [ *softens* ] <note>original: "mollifie" — to soften or make supple, specifically referring here to the melting of the "hard" or "harsh" first quality.</note> what was hard, thins what was thick, and makes luminous what was dark.

salitter <term>Salitter: Böhme's specific term for the divine, celestial substance or "saltpeter" of heaven from which all things are made.</term>; for he penetrated everywhere with the fury and ardor of a madman, and imagined that he was the only *God*, and that he would reign through his biting <note>original: "mordicante" — a sharp, stinging, or corrosive quality.</note> quality.

This is also why His generation <note>original: "engendrement" — the process by which divine nature manifests or "gives birth" to its own qualities.</note> can be grasped, whereas the spirits of *Lucifer* are tearing and bubble up in an astringent, burning, and impetuous manner.

**53.** You have an example of this in the ignited *salitter* of the stars, which, because of this inflamed fury, must thus circulate violently with vanity until the last judgment; and then the harshness shall be separated from them and shall be given to King *Lucifer* for an eternal dwelling.

**54.** That there is a great opposition to this within *God* needs no testimony; but a man may consider, in the event that a similar source of choleric <note>In historical medicine, the "choleric" temperament was associated with fire, bile, and anger.</note> fire should rise up in his love, what opposition and what disgust he would experience, and how often his whole body would be irritated.

**55.** This is, in effect, what happens to those who give shelter within themselves to the demon. While he is only a guest, he remains quiet like a little tame dog; but if he becomes the master of the inn, he then ravages the house, just as he did with the body of *God*.

## CHAPTER SIXTEEN.

### Concerning the seventh type, or form of the beginning of sin in Lucifer and his angels.

1. Here you may indeed open your eyes, for you will see secret things that have been hidden from all men since the beginning of the world; indeed, you will see the murderous cavern of the demon, and the horrible sin, enmity, and perdition.

2. The demon taught sorcery to men in order to strengthen his reign; but if he had revealed to men the true foundation upon which it rests, there are many who would have left it alone.

3. Approach, you diviners and sorcerers who prostitute yourselves with the demon; come to my school. I wish to show you how your <term>necromantic art<note>original: "art nécromancien"; for Böhme, this refers to any attempt to manipulate the spiritual world through the dark "fountain-spirits" or the "abyss" rather than through divine light.</note></term> leads you into hell. You take pleasure in having the demon submit to you, and you think you are Gods; here I will expose the origin of necromancy to you, for I have also become expert in <term>nature<note>Böhme uses "nature" to describe the internal workings of the "seven qualities" of the universe, which he believes can be understood through spiritual revelation.</note></term>; not, it is true, in your manner, but to discover your shame, through a divine revelation, as a warning for these last days, and as a

For the astringent quality draws together the <term>salitter<note>original: "salitter"; a unique term used by Böhme to describe the "divine saltpeter" or the essential "sap" of the universe—the substance from which all things are formed.</note></term>, or the operation of all six spirits, just as a magnet draws the *salitter* of iron to itself; and when this is drawn together, there is a [*tangibility*], in which the six spirits of God <term>inter-operate<note>original: "inqualifient"; Böhme’s term for spirits working within one another to manifest their qualities.</note></term> in an intangible way.

It is also from this spirit that all configurations and all forms take their character; furthermore, the angels are also formed from it; and everything concerning [*naturalization*] exists within it.

7. This same spirit is unceasingly generated from the other six, and it subsists eternally, and never passes away. On the contrary, it in turn perpetually generates the six spirits within this seventh: for the other six are enclosed as if within a mother, and they unceasingly draw their nourishment, their force, and their power from the *body* of their mother.

17. Rather, it must remain at rest like a generated body, and let the <term>source-veins</term> <note>original: "veines sources"; the spiritual conduits or channels through which the divine life-force flows.</note>—that is, the spirits—in-qualify and work within it according to their will. For it is the property or the dwelling of the six spirits, which build it continually according to their taste, like a garden of delights in which the master of the house sows every kind of fruit according to his good pleasure, and from which he takes his enjoyment.

### CH. 16.

18. Thus the six other spirits continually build this garden of delights; they sow their fruits there, and nourish themselves upon them for the increase of their strength and their joy; and this is the garden where the angels dwell and walk, and in which the celestial fruit grows.

19. But the wonderful harmony shown in the productions and forms of this garden comes from the *in-qualifying* <note>As noted previously, this refers to the way the divine qualities permeate and work within one another.</note>, or the love-play of the other spirits: for whichever one holds superiority in the struggle is the one that shapes the production according to its own mode; furthermore, the others help it continually; sometimes it is one that occupies the post, sometimes another, sometimes a third, and so on.

20. This is why a great multitude of productions and figures arise, which is entirely elusive and incomprehensible to the corporeal reason of the angel; but perfectly intelligible to the *soul-reason of that same angel* <note>The "corporeal reason" refers to understanding based on outer form, while "soul-reason" refers to the inner, spiritual understanding granted by the divine light.</note>.

21. This is likewise hidden from my body, but not from my soul-spirit. It understands this as long as it *in-qualifies* with God; but when it falls into sin, the door is closed and bolted against it by the demon <note>original: "démon"; Böhme often uses this to refer to the devil or the spirit of pride and wrath that opposes the light.</note>; and it requires a great labor of the spirit to reopen it.

22. I know well that the demon's wrath will cover this revelation with mockery in many impious hearts, for it fills him with confusion. He has also launched several assaults upon my soul regarding this matter; but I leave the direction of this to the one who disposes of me. I cannot resist him; and even if my body—

( 61 ) CH. 16:

...have set forth many questions and opinions regarding the terrible evil that exists in all creatures, as well as in the sun and the stars of this world. Furthermore, there are in this world certain malicious animals, worms, and poisonous plants which have rightly provoked the surprise of observers. There are some who have concluded from this that God must have also willed evil, since He had created so many evil things; some attribute it to the Fall of man; and some others to the operation of the demon.

29. But since all creatures and all plants were created before the formation of man, he should not be held at fault for them; for man did not receive his animal body <note>In the author's mystical theology—likely influenced by Jacob Böhme—man's "animal body" refers to our current physical, mortal state, which is distinct from the "celestial" or "spiritual" body man supposedly possessed before the Fall.</note> at the moment of his creation, but it was given to him at the instant of his fall. Likewise, man did not bring evil and poison into the animals, the birds, the worms, and the stones, for he did not possess their bodies. Otherwise, if he had brought wrath <note>original: "colère" — in this context, it refers to a cosmic or divine quality of fire and judgment that became "unbalanced" or "poisonous" due to rebellion.</note> into all creatures, he would never have obtained grace from God, which is the case with the demons. Unfortunate man did not fall by a resolution of his own will, but by the poisonous influence of the demon. Otherwise, there would have been no resource <note>Meaning no hope for salvation or "re-source-ing" back to the divine.</note> for him.

30. Now, you will find below this true instruction described, not by a desire to disparage anyone, but out of love, as a humble teaching drawn from the abyss of my spirit <note>original: "l'abîme de mon esprit" — implying the deepest, most foundational part of the soul where the author believes he receives divine revelation.</note>, and as a steady support for every old...

<summary>The author argues that the evil found in the natural world predates humanity and thus cannot be blamed on the Fall of man, but rather on demonic influence. 40. 51. 52. 53. judgment of God; then he will receive, as an eternal bath, the fire of wrath separated from love, and he will most certainly be flooded by his own sorcery.

55. 56. ->*On the igniting of the fire of wrath.*<-

57. When, therefore, King Lucifer ignited himself with all his angels, the fire instantly rose within his body; and the gracious light was extinguished in the soul-spirit, and became a furious demonic spirit, all according to the ignition and the will of the source-spirits <term>source-spirits: the seven fundamental qualities (Quellgeister) such as "astringency" or "bitterness" that Böhme believes constitute the life-force of nature</term>.

58. Now, this soul-spirit was linked with the divinity in nature, and could co-qualify <term>co-qualify: (French: *inqualifier*) to harmonize or operate with the same essential properties and powers as another being</term> with it, as if they were but one thing; it pierced from the body of the demon into the nature of...

<summary>This passage describes the spiritual consequences for those who practice sorcery, framing it as a misalignment with God's love that leads to eternal wrath. It then begins to recount the fall of Lucifer, explaining how his rebellion "ignited" a fire that transformed his soul-spirit from light into a demonic fury.</summary>

<header>CH. 16. ( 69 )</header>

God, like a murderer and a thief who wanted to exterminate everything, steal everything, and subject everything to his power; and he ignited all seven spirits within nature. Then there was nothing but tearing, devastation, astringency, bitterness, and a crackling, burning fire. <note>The author describes Lucifer's rebellion as an attempt to seize the divine mechanisms of creation for himself, which resulted in the "ignition" of the harsh, elemental qualities of nature.</note>

59. One must not think that the demon thus subjected the divinity to his power. No; rather, he ignited the wrath of God which had been hidden in rest throughout eternity; and he transformed the divine <term>salitter: the fundamental "saltpeter" or divine substance that serves as the "soil" of the heavenly realm</term> into a den of murder; for if fire enters into flammable substances, they burn—but God did not, for that reason, become a demon.

60. The fire of God's wrath also does not reach into the innermost core of nature, which is the Son of God, and even less into the hidden holiness of the spirit; but only into the generation of the six <term>source-spirits: the fundamental qualities (such as bitterness, heat, or sound) that Böhme believes constitute the life-force of all existence</term>, in the place where the seventh is generated.

61. For it is in this place, or in this generation, that Lucifer became a creature; and his dominion reaches no further. But if he had remained in love, then his <term>soul-spirit: the "esprit animique," or the active, spiritual essence of the soul</term> would have reached into the very center of the heart of God, for love penetrates through the entirety of the divinity.

62. Thus, when his love was extinguished, his soul-spirit could no longer pierce into the heart of God, and his attempt was useless; instead, he brought devastation and storm into nature—that is to say, into the seventh source-spirit of God.

<summary>This page explains that Lucifer’s fall did not corrupt the core of God, but rather "ignited" the latent wrath within the outer layers of divine nature. It distinguishes between the innermost holy core of the divinity and the "source-spirits" where Lucifer's influence is contained.</summary>

63. Now, since the power of all seven spirits resided in this one [*the seventh*], then all seven were also stirred into wrath, but only in their most external and most tangible quality. 64. At that very moment, King Lucifer prepared for himself hell and eternal perdition, which now consists in the outermost source-spirit of the nature of God, or rather in the outermost <term>generation: the process by which spiritual substance is "born" or formed</term> of this world.

### CH. 16. ( 71 )

66. ...ly dark and corrupt; the water became entirely cold and thick, and settled somewhat into the crevices. This is the origin of elemental water on the earth.

67. For before the birth of this world, water was as fluid as air. Furthermore, it is in this water—which today is so mortal and so corrupt, and which flows here and there—that life is engendered.

68. From that same gracious love which rose up in the flash of life, there arose a furious and bitter poison; a true cavern of murder; a sting of death. The tone <note>In Böhme’s thought, "tone" or "sound" refers to the vibrational quality of a divine spirit</note> became like the harsh [ <gloss>clashing</gloss> ] of stones; a house of sorrow.

69. In a word, everything became an entirely dark and suffering substance throughout the whole region, in the outermost generation of Lucifer’s kingdom.

70. However, one must not believe that nature was thus corrupted and ignited down to its innermost base; it was only the outermost generation <note>the visible, material manifestation</note> that was affected. But the interior—that in which the <term>source-spirits: the seven fundamental divine qualities or "Quellgeister" that drive all existence</term> are engendered—preserves its rights, since the ignited demon could not penetrate there.

71. Now, the interior generation has the winnowing fan in hand, and one day it will purge its threshing floor and give the husks to King Lucifer for his eternal nourishment <note>An allusion to the biblical metaphor of separating wheat from chaff; here, Lucifer is left with only the "husks" or the empty, dead remnants of reality</note>. For if the demon had been able to penetrate the innermost generation, then the entire region of his kingdom would have become a flaming and burning abyss.

76. Thus, come forward, you defenders of Lucifer; prove that your king Lucifer had the right to ignite the fire of wrath in nature; otherwise, he must burn in this fire eternally, and your lies against the truth along with him.

### *Of the final battle, and of the expulsion of King Lucifer, as well as all his angels.*

91. ...against the seventh of the *source-spirits* <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "sources-esprits"; likely a translation of the German "Quellgeister," referring to the seven fundamental properties or forces that constitute nature and the soul in this mystical system.</note> which engender the soul; and this with the intention of destroying it and setting it on fire, so that he might obtain as his own property the entire *body* or *circumscription*.

100. Now, if the soul wishes to carry its light and its knowledge into the *affective basis* <term>affective basis</term> <note>original: "base affective"; the seat of human emotions and desires where the spiritual struggle is most visceral.</note> of man, it must sustain a harsh *combat*; the *passage* <note>An allusion to the "strait gate" or narrow way of spiritual salvation.</note> into it is very narrow; it is often *thrown down* by the demon; but it must *remain firm* like a *hero* in the battle. If it prevails, the demon is subdued; but if it is the demon who triumphs, then the soul is a prisoner.

101. But since this *generation of flesh* <term>generation of flesh</term> <note>original: "géniture de chair"; the physical, mortal body resulting from the Fall, which the author views as a temporary shell rather than the soul's true home.</note> is not the soul's own dwelling, and since it cannot possess it by inheritance like the demon, the combat lasts just as long as the *house of flesh* exists; but when the *house of flesh* is destroyed (provided the soul is not yet defeated nor imprisoned in its house, and remains free there), then the combat is at its end, and the demon must forever depart from this spirit.

102. This is why this is a very difficult point to understand, and can absolutely only be understood within this combat. I could write many books about it, yet you would not understand it, unless your spirit were in a similar *birthing process* <note>original: "enfantement"; the author suggests that spiritual knowledge cannot be taught, but must be "born" through the labor of personal struggle and experience.</note>, and knowledge of it were engendered within yourself; without this, you can neither grasp nor believe it.

103. But if you understand it, you also understand the combat that the angels sustained against the demon; for the angels have neither flesh nor bones, and

104. 105. Such is the extent of the region they hold at present until the last judgment; then they will receive a dwelling in the place where the earth currently is (that is, in the outermost generation, in the darkness, where they will not reach the second principle <note>In Boehme's cosmology, the "second principle" represents the world of light and divine love.</note> or the source of light), and that shall be called the burning hell.

106. 107. However, it will not be in the same form as it is currently; but everything will be subdivided by the blazing of the fire of wrath; and it will be a dark desert, burning and cold, harsh, and bitter...

## CHAPTER SEVENTEEN.

->On the lamentable and painful state of corrupted nature; and the origin of the four elements, in place of the holy government of God.<-

1. Although God is an eternal and almighty governor whom no one can resist, nature—in its <term>ignition</term> <note>original: "embrâsement." This refers to the state where the spiritual forces of nature were "set on fire" or agitated by Lucifer's rebellion.</note>—has nevertheless received a most astonishing regime which had not existed before the time of the <term>wrath</term> <note>original: "colère." In this theology, this signifies the "dark" or "harsh" principle of divine power (Grimmigkeit) that becomes manifest when light and love are rejected.</note>.

2. For, before the time of the wrath, the six <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "sources-esprits." These are the seven fundamental qualities or forces (such as bitterness, sweetness, heat, etc.) that Jacob Boehme describes as the building blocks of both divine and earthly nature.</note> gently and graciously generated the seventh spirit of nature within the space of this world, just as it happens now in heaven; and absolutely no spark of wrath arose there.

3. Furthermore, everything there was universally light, and there was no need for any other brightness; rather, the <term>bubbling fountain</term> <note>original: "fontaine bouillonnante." A recurring image in this work representing the overflowing, generative life of God’s heart.</note> of the heart of God illuminated everything. There was a light in all things that shone everywhere without interruption and without obstacle; for nature was entirely rarefied; everything consisted only of powers and was a most lovely <term>temperature</term> <note>original: "température." Here used in its older sense of a perfect "temperament" or a harmonious balance where no single quality dominates or conflicts with another.</note>.

4. But as soon as the battle with the foolish demon <note>A reference to Lucifer.</note> began within nature, then in the

<summary>This page describes the transition of the universe from a primordial state of perfect, harmonious light to a corrupted state of "wrath" following Lucifer’s rebellion. It explains that the physical world we know was originally a spiritual realm governed by the "seven source-spirits" in perfect balance.</summary>
<keywords>corrupted nature, four elements, source-spirits, spirit of nature, divine light, wrath of God, Lucifer, cosmology</keywords>

seventh spirit of nature, in the region of Lucifer—which is the place of this world—everything took on another form and changed its operation.

5. For nature has acquired a <term>double source</term> <note>This refers to the "dualistic" nature of our world, which contains both the potential for divine light and the reality of harsh, "wrathful" struggle.</note>, and every outermost <term>generation</term> <note>original: "engendrement." In this context, it refers to the way the spiritual forces "give birth" to or produce physical manifestations.</note> in nature has been ignited in the fire of wrath, which fire is now called the wrath of God, or the burning hell.

Here, the deepest penetration is required to understand this. Only the place where the light is generated in the heart can comprehend it. The <term>outer man</term> <note>A common mystical term for the physical, unspiritual side of a human being that relies only on the five senses.</note> does not understand it.

6. See. When Lucifer with his legions awakened the fire of wrath in the nature of God, until it provoked God in nature within Lucifer's place, then the outermost generation in nature acquired another quality—entirely fiery, <term>astringent</term> <note>original: "astringente." One of the primary forces in Boehme's system, representing a "contracting" or "binding" power.</note>, cold, hot, bitter, and sour. The bubbling spirit which previously had <term>operated</term> <note>original: "inqualifié." This describes how a spirit acts or interacts according to its internal qualities.</note> very sweetly in nature, became exalted and terrible in its outer generation; this is what, in the outermost generation, is now called the wind, or the air because of its fury.

7. For when the seven spirits were ignited in their outermost generation, they thus generated a bubbling spirit with violence; and also the sweet water—which before the time of wrath was subtle and entirely intangible—became thick and swollen, and the astringent quality became entirely sharp and similar to a fire.

8. In effect, salt water, or the salt found even today in the earth, has its origin and its principle in the first ignition <note>original: "enflammement." Stones also derive their source and their birth from this, as well as the earth.

9. Boehme believed sound or "tone" was a formative power in nature that gave shape to matter.</note>.

10. For as nature found itself with its operation, its struggle, and the expansion of its birthing at the moment of the ignition, so also was the conglomerated substance.

->How did a perceptible son proceed from an imperceptible mother?<-

...is generated <note>original: "gendre." 14. Likewise, it is in this same way that the earth and stones took their formation: for when the <term>salitter</term> <note>Boehme’s term for the fundamental cosmic substance or "divine sap" from which all things are made.</note> was ignited in nature, everything became harsh, thick, and dark, like an obscure and dense cloud; and the astringent quality dried it all through its coldness.

15. But since the light was extinguished in the outermost generation <note>The physical or most external level of reality.</note>, then the heat was imprisoned in <term>tangibility</term> <note>original: "saisissabilité." This refers to the state where spiritual forces become fixed, solid, and "graspable" by the senses.</note>, and no longer had the power to generate its life. This, then, is how death entered into nature; it is because nature and the corrupted earth could no longer support *the heat*; and consequently, another creation of light had to follow, otherwise the earth would have been an eternal, indissoluble death: but now it generates its fruits through the virtue and the ignition of the created light.

->Now one might ask:<-

How did this form through this double generation?

Did God extinguish Himself in the ignition of the fire of wrath in the space of this world, so that nothing remains but a fire of wrath, or have two Gods emerged from one?

->Response.<-

16. You cannot better grasp and understand this than in your own body, which, by the first fall of Adam, has become—with all its workings <note>original: "engendremens." This refers to the internal processes or "birthings" of thoughts and physical drives.</note>, talents, and desires—a dwelling similar to what the state of this world has become.

17. You have, first of all, the animal flesh which became so through *lustful eating* <term>manducation</term> <note>The formal act of eating. Boehme uses this to describe how the desire to consume physical matter changed Adam's nature.</note>: for it is the house of corruption.

18. When Adam was formed from the corrupted <term>salitter</term> <note>As noted previously, this is Boehme’s term for the fundamental "divine sap" or cosmic substance. Instead, he had a mighty angelic body in which he was to exist eternally; and he was to eat of the angelic fruit which also grew for him in paradise before his fall, before the Lord cursed the earth.

19. But as the seed or the mass from which Adam was formed was somewhat infected by the sickness of the demon <note>Referring to the lingering effects of Lucifer's rebellion on the material of the universe.</note>, Adam then directed his desires toward his mother—that is to say, he wanted to eat of the fruit of the corrupted earth, which then, in its most external <term>tangibility</term> <note>original: "saisissabilité." The state of being solid or graspable by the senses, rather than purely spiritual.</note>, had become evil, and in the <term>wrathful fire</term> <note>original: "feu colérique." In Boehme’s cosmology, this is the harsh, consuming fire of God’s justice or "wrath" that becomes dominant when light is withdrawn.</note>, had thus become palpable and coarse.

20. Now, as the spirit of Adam directed its desires toward a similar fruit which was of the quality of the corrupted earth, then nature shaped for him a fruit of the same quality as the earth...

<meta>This page marks a transition from the spiritual struggle of the soul discussed in the previous chapter to a cosmogonic reflection on the origins of the world. ## CHAPTER EIGHTEEN.

### On the creation of heaven and earth, and the first day.

1. MOSES speaks of this in his first book <note>The Book of Genesis, the first book of the Pentateuch.</note> as if he had been there and had seen it himself. Without doubt, he received it in writing from his predecessors; he may also have gone further in spirit regarding this than his predecessors.

2. But since, at the time when God created heaven and earth, there was as yet no man to witness it, one must conclude that Adam, before his fall, while he was still in the profound knowledge of God, knew this in spirit. But when he fell and was established in the most external *engendering* <term>engendering</term> <note>original: "engendrement." In this mystical context, it refers to the process by which reality is "born" or manifested into the physical, material realm from spiritual foundations.</note>, he no longer knew this, but preserved only an obscure memory of it, like a veiled story, and transmitted it to his descendants.

3. Furthermore, it is manifest that the first world before the flood knew as little as the last one in which we live about the divine qualities and birthing; for carnal and outermost generation has never been able to grasp or understand the divinity; otherwise, something more would have been written about it.

4. But since, by divine grace, the great

<summary>This page discusses the origins of the Book of Genesis, suggesting that Moses's knowledge of Creation was a combination of inherited tradition and spiritual insight originally possessed by Adam before the Fall. The author argues that material humanity is naturally incapable of understanding divine processes without such spiritual intervention.</summary>

8. But since I have the impulse for it, I let the one who knows and understands what it is—and who wills that the thing proceed thus—act and operate. As for me, a man of dust and earth, I could do nothing on my own.

9. Otherwise, they will be vomited out like the filth of hell into the most exterior generation, within the wrath of God.

->*Now observe.*<-

10. When God was provoked to anger <note>Original: "Dieu fut irrité." In the theology of Jacob Boehme, the "wrath" or "anger" of God refers to a specific state where the fiery, harsh qualities of nature are "kindled" and lose their balance with the light.</note> in the third **generation** <term>generation</term> <note>Boehme uses the term "generation" (engendrement) to describe the ongoing process by which divine forces manifest into being.</note> in the region of Lucifer—which was then the entire place and space of this world—the light was then extinguished in the third generation and all became darkness. 11. For in the place where the **astringent** <note>The "astringent" quality is the force of contraction and cold; in Boehme’s system, it is the first of the seven source-spirits and is responsible for solidity.</note> quality held sway, there the salliter was compressed and dried out, so that hard and compact stones arose from it. But in the places where the astringent spirit and the bitter spirit held sway at the same time, there arose sharp sand, for the fiery bitter spirit broke the salliter.

38. But if a wild branch is planted in favorable soil, and furthermore, if it is grafted onto another plant of a useful quality and good taste, then a sweet tree results from it, even though the branch was wild. For here, all is possible; the good changes as easily into evil as the evil into good.
