...fruits, and would never bear good ones. But since the earth is still within the love of God, His wrath will not burn there eternally; instead, the love that has triumphed will cast out the fire of wrath.

**41.** Then the burning hell will begin, in which love will separate from wrath; but in this world, love and wrath exist within every creature. Whichever of the two prevails in the struggle inherits the house by right of conquest, whether it be the kingdom of heaven or that of hell.

**42.** I do not mean by this that animals, through their reproduction, must inherit the kingdom of heaven, for they are like the corrupted earth—a mix of good and evil. But when they are sown again into their mother, which is the earth, they simply become earth.

**43.** However, this same <term>salitter<note>Original French: *salitter*. This is a central term in Jacob Boehme’s mystical chemistry, likely derived from "salt-nitre." It refers to the divine, creative substance or "spiritual sap" that constitutes the heavenly landscape and the essential qualities of nature.</note></term> in a good animal will not be given to the demon as his property for that reason. Instead, it will flourish eternally within God’s nature, in its own separate domain. That is to say, its form will remain like a shadow upon the holy earth, among the wonders—specifically, within <term>eternal magic<note>Original French: *éternelle magie*. For Boehme, "magic" is not sorcery but the spiritual "will" or formative power that brings ideas into being and gives shape to the divine essence.</note></term>—and will give rise to other celestial forms. Conversely, the *salitter* of animals belonging to God’s wrath will eternally bear infernal fruits within that wrath.

**44.** For when the earth is set ablaze, the fire will burn in wrath, and the light in love. Everything will be separated; indeed, one will never be able to grasp the other again. But in this present time, everything has a dual source; what you sow here...

<summary>This page describes the eventual cosmic separation of divine love and wrath, explaining that while animals do not have immortal souls like humans, their essential qualities (salitter) are preserved as "shadows" in either the heavenly or infernal realms based on their nature.</summary>

### CH. 18. ( 101 )

...in spirit or that you build, whether with words, works, or thoughts, will become your eternal dwelling.

**45.** Thus you see from where the earth and stones have originated; but if this same inflamed <term>salitter<note>A term unique to Jacob Boehme, referring to the fundamental "divine sap" or spiritual substance of nature. Here, he describes it as having been "inflamed" or corrupted by the fall of Lucifer.</note></term> had been required to remain thus in the universal space of this world, then this entire place would have been a valley of darkness; for the light had been imprisoned in the <term>third generation<note>In Boehme’s complex system, the "third generation" refers to the physical, material world—the most external realm of existence which followed the two purely spiritual realms.</note></term>.

**46.** The light of God’s heart in its innermost generation was not imprisoned because of this. But its radiance in the third engendering was incorporated into the outermost <gloss>graspability</gloss>, which is why men love all the things that are in this same *salitter*.

**47.** But as the entire space in the third generation had become dark, because of the corrupted *salitter* of the earth and stones, then the divinity could not suffer it to be so; but it created the earth and the stones at once, into a mass.

Now, Moses writes of this:

> In the beginning, God created the heaven and the earth ( *Gen. 1* ).

->IN THE BEGINNING GOD CREATED HEAVEN AND EARTH.<-

**48.** One must observe these words exactly and see what they are in themselves. ### CH. 18. ( 102 )

**49.** Now, this signifies that the sound has gone out from the heart of God and has embraced the universal space of this world; but as this space was found to be evil, the sound then returned to its place.

**50.** The word ***an*** <note>The first syllable of the German word *Anfang*, meaning "beginning." Boehme uses "speech-chemistry," believing the physical movement of the mouth when speaking reveals divine truths.</note> launches itself from the heart to the mouth and leaves a long trace; but when it is pronounced, it shuts itself in the middle, in its seat, against the upper part of the palate, and is half outside and half inside.

**51.** This signifies that the heart of God felt a loathing for the corruption and repelled the corrupted being from itself; but that He holds it embraced once more in the middle, through the heart.

**52.** Just as the tongue divides the speech and holds it half outside and half inside, so too the heart of God would not wish to entirely reject the inflamed <term>salitter<note>Boehme’s term for the fundamental "divine sap" or spiritual substance of nature, which here has been "inflamed" or tainted by evil.</note></term>; but rather that the malice and corruption of the demon, and that which is *changed*, might be restored once more after this time.

**53.** The word ***fang*** <note>The second syllable of *Anfang*. In German, *fangen* also means "to catch" or "to capture."</note> goes rapidly from the heart to the mouth and is also held back by the palate at the lower part of the tongue; and when it is released, it makes another rapid compression from the heart to the mouth.

**54.** This signifies the promptness of the repulsion and the displacement of the demon, as well as of the corrupted *salitter*: for the powerful and prompt spirit strongly pushes its breath out from itself, yet retains within itself, in the back part of the palate, the true tone of the word or its pronunciation; that is to say, the true spirit of the word.

**55.** This signifies that the corrupted fury <note>Original: "fureur." In Boehme's theology, this refers to the *Grimm* or the "wrath" of God—the harsh, dark quality of nature that became dominant after the fall of the angels.</note> will be

...eternally rejected from the light of God; but the inner spirit which is oppressed by it against its will, shall be restored to its first dwelling place.

**56.** The final compression ***ang*** <note>The syllable "ang" is the root of the German word *Angst*, meaning "anguish" or "constriction." Boehme views this as the tightening of spiritual forces.</note> signifies that, as a consequence of perdition, even the innermost spirits are not entirely pure, and for that reason need to be purified, and that the wrath is consumed in the fire, which will happen at the end of this time.

**57.** The word ***schuf*** <note>German for "created."</note> compacts itself above and below the tongue; it brings the teeth together with the upper and lower parts of the palate, and thus compresses itself; and when it is compressed and pronounced, then the mouth opens again with speed, like a flash of lightning.

**58.** This signifies that the astringent spirit of the corrupted <term>salitter<note>Boehme’s term for the fundamental "divine sap" or spiritual substance of nature, which can exist in either a pure or a "corrupted" (inflamed) state.</note></term> has been strongly tightened into a mass. For the teeth hold back the word, yet allow the spirit to pass through freely. This signifies that the astringent quality holds the earth and stones firmly in coagulation, and yet allows the spirits of the earth to grow and flourish from the astringent spirit, which indicates the generation and restoration of the spirits of the earth.

**59.** But the mouth quickly reopens following the word; this signifies the space above the earth, which God nevertheless still wishes to inhabit, and for which He wishes to reserve the government for Himself, holding the demon there as a prisoner in the fire of wrath.

**60.** The word ***gott*** <note>German for "God."</note> is conceived in the middle, upon the tongue, and leaps out from the heart; it causes [the mouth] to open...

**61.** This signifies that when God created the heaven and the earth, and furthermore all creatures, he nonetheless remained in his divine, eternal, almighty seat, and never departed from it, and that he alone is all. The final compression signifies the subtlety of his spirit, by which he accomplishes everything in an instant, within his entire *circumscription.* <note>Boehme uses "circumscription" to refer to the divine boundary or the sphere of God's immediate presence and activity.</note>

**63.** This signifies that the innermost generation <note>In Boehme’s thought, "generation" refers to the continuous process of birth or manifestation within the divine nature.</note> has been closed off by the outermost, because of terrible sin, and remains incomprehensible to this corrupted outer generation.

### CH. 18.

...clear divinity, and that through this the Holy Spirit was to rise again in this world from both sides—that is, from the most inward depth of the Father and the Son—and that this world was to be regenerated anew by the new King.

**66.** The word *und* <note>original: "und"; German for "and."</note> is conceived in the heart and is gathered and compacted by the tongue against the upper part of the palate; when it is released, it creates another compression from the heart to the mouth. Now, this signifies the difference between the heavenly <term>generation</term> <note>Boehme uses "generation" to describe the continuous process of birth or manifestation within the divine nature or the natural world.</note> and the earthly generation.

**67.** The syllable indeed leaps from the heart, but it is held back by the tongue in the upper part of the palate, so that one does not hear what word it is; this signifies that the earthly and corrupted generation cannot grasp the innermost generation, but rather dwells in madness and blindness.

**68.** The final compression of the heart signifies that this earthly generation may well, in its *sensory manifestation*, <term>interpenetrate</term> <note>original: "inqualifier." This is Boehme’s term for the way spiritual forces or "qualities" (Qualitäten) act within and through each other to produce a specific effect or state of being.</note> with its most inward generation; but its instinct cannot comprehend it. This is why the syllable is mute and lacks discernment, and is used only to designate the difference.

**69.** The word *erden* <note>original: "erden"; German for "earths" or "to the earth."</note> leaps from the heart and compacts itself at the back of the tongue, in the posterior part of the palate, and it trembles; nevertheless, the tongue does not use the first syllable *er*; instead, it curves inward toward the lower gums and huddles there as if before an enemy.

Following the analysis of the word "und" (and) on the previous page, Boehme now breaks down the two syllables of the word "erden" (earth) to explain how its phonetic articulation mirrors the spiritual state of the physical world—both its fallen material shell and its inner heart, which God intends to redeem.</meta>

### CH. 18

**70.** The second syllable *den* <note>Boehme is analyzing the German word *erden* (earth). The syllable *den* is the second half of the word.</note> is compacted with the tongue and the upper part of the palate, and it makes the mouth open; the spirit of formation goes out through the organ of respiration <note>Referring to the nose or the breath.</note>, and can no longer, in this word, go out through the mouth; and although something does come out of it, nevertheless the true sound of the genuine spirit only goes out through the organ of smell and respiration.

->*This is a great mystery.*<-

**71.** The word *er* <note>The first syllable of *erden*.</note> signifies the astringent and bitter qualities set on fire—the severe wrath of God—which shudders in the back part of the palate; at this, the tongue is terrified; and it curls back into the lower part of the palate, and flees as if before an enemy.

**72.** The word *den* is compacted once again upon the tongue, and the spirit draws the power out of the word, and carries itself thereby, through another way, toward the organ of respiration: thus it ascends through there into the brain, before the royal seat <note>Boehme often refers to the brain or the seat of the mind as the "royal seat" or "throne" of human consciousness and divine reflection.</note>.

**73.** This signifies that the outermost <term>salitter</term> <note>A term coined by Boehme, derived from "saltpeter," used to describe the divine "sap" or "matter" of the spiritual world that constitutes the environment of heaven or the foundational substance of nature.</note> of the earth is rejected forever from the light and holiness of God.

**74.** But because the spirit embraces the power of the word, and carries itself by another way through the organ of respiration into the brain, before the seat of thoughts, this signifies that God wants to withdraw the heart of the earth from fury and wickedness, and employ it for the praise of His eternal <term>kingship</term> <note>original: "royauté." In this context, it refers to God's sovereign rule and the divine order of the universe.</note>.

**75.** He will withdraw from the earth the kernel <note>The "kernel" or "nucleus" represents the essential, spiritual part of creation that remains after the corruption of the material world is stripped away.</note>, or that which is best, the good spirit, and will beget it anew for his praise and for his glory.

**76.** Here, O men, consider what kind of grain you sow in the earth; it is this same grain that will rise again and will eternally bear flowers and fruits, whether in love, or in wrath <note>Boehme describes "Love" and "Wrath" (or the "Dark Fire") as the two eternal principles or "centers" of existence.</note>.

**77.** But when the good shall be separated from the evil, then you will live in the portion you have acquired for yourself, whether in heaven, or in the infernal fire. That in which you operate, that is where your soul goes when you die.

**78.** Do you think that my spirit has drawn this from the corrupted earth, or from refuse materials? No, in truth. But in those moments when I wrote, my spirit interacted <note>original: "inqualifié". This is a French rendering of Boehme's technical term *mitqualificiren*, meaning his spirit was actively co-operating or resonating with the divine qualities.</note> with the deepest begetting of God. It is there that I took my knowledge; it is from there that it was drawn, not in great earthly joy, but in a painful labor <note>original: "enfantement douloureux" (painful childbirth). Boehme frequently uses the imagery of birth to describe the process of spiritual revelation.</note>, and in anguish.

**79.** For, unless you pass through a similar trial, you would not understand what I have had to suffer from the demon and the infernal quality, which reigns in my outer man, as in all men.

### *Of the creation of light in this world.*

81. Here, close the eyes of your flesh for a moment, for they will be of no use to you, since they are blind and dead; and open the eyes of your spirit, and then I will show you exactly the creation of God.

82. When, therefore, God had gathered into a mass the corrupted *salitter* <note>A term unique to Boehme, derived from "Sal Nitrum." It refers to the fundamental "salt" or spiritual matter of the divine realm which, in our world, has become corrupted or acidified.</note> of the earth and the stones, which had resulted from the most external *begetting* <note>original: "engendrement." 83. ### AND THERE WAS DARKNESS UPON THE DEEP. (Genesis 1). <note>original: "UND ES WAR FINSTER AUF DER TIEFFE"</note>

[ *See my note, chapter 8, verse 74* ].

The word *auf* [upon] signifies the most external begetting <note>Boehme uses "begetting" (génération/engendrement) to describe the process of cosmic manifestation—how one quality or realm is born from another.</note>, and the word *in* signifies the most internal begetting.

85. But if the most internal begetting had been dark, then the wrath of God would have remained eternally in this world, and it would never have had light; however, the wrath did not thus reach the heart of God.

86. That is why he said: let there be light. HE SPOKE: LET THERE BE LIGHT. <note>original: "SPRACH ER : ES WERDE LIECHT"</note>

87. The word *Sprach* [Spoke] is used here in a human manner. You, philosophers, open your eyes; in my simplicity, I wish to teach you the language of God as it must be.

<meta>This page continues the mystical and phonetic analysis of the word "Sprach" (Spoke). In the previous page, Boehme described how the spirit (breath) moves from the heart and palate through the "wrath" of the throat. Here, he interprets the sound produced by the teeth as a symbol of the Holy Spirit's movement through the dark, material world during creation.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>CH. 18.</header>

92. Now, see, son of man <note>An address to the reader as a representative of humanity, echoing biblical prophets like Ezekiel.</note>, what this gate of the heavens, of hell, and of the earth is, and even of the whole divinity that the spirit opens to you.

93. It must not be thought that in those times God spoke in the manner of a man, as if it were only a powerless word, similar to that of a human. The word of man also compacts itself <note>Boehme uses the physical formation of sounds in the mouth as a map of how the universe was created.</note> in the same form, proportion, quality, and skill; only man, being half dead, does not understand it: and this understanding is altogether sublime and precious, for it is only birthed in the knowledge of the Holy Spirit.

94. But the word of God, that which He pronounced then in His power, embraces heaven and earth, and the heaven of all heavens, and the entire divinity.

95. But it first compacts itself between the teeth held together, and it sibilates <note>original: "siffle." This refers to the "s" sound at the start of the word "Sprach," which creates a hissing or whistling sound through the teeth.</note>. This signifies that the Holy Spirit, at the beginning of creation, passed through the thick, closed wall of the third and outermost generation <term>generation: in Boehme's system, a stage of cosmic manifestation or "birth"</term> that exists in the wrath of this world; for it is written: *and darkness was upon the face of the deep, and the Spirit of God moved upon the waters.*

96. The depth signifies the innermost generation; and the darkness signifies the corrupted and outermost generation, in which the wrath burned. The water signifies the calming of the spirit.

97. But that the spirit sibilates through the teeth signifies that the spirit has passed from the heart of God through the wrath; but that the teeth re- <note>The text cuts off mid-word, continuing the description of the teeth as a barrier or gate through which the divine breath must pass.</note>

<summary>Boehme interprets the physical act of hissing through the teeth as a mystical representation of the Holy Spirit penetrating the "wall" of worldly wrath during creation. He contrasts the living, creative Word of God with the "half-dead" speech of fallen humanity.</summary>

100. 101. This further signifies that the holy spirit also has an open gate in the house of wrath of this world, where it performs its work in a manner incomprehensible to the hellish gates, and where it prepares for itself a holy seed for its eternal praise, without the will of the powerful hellish gates, and entirely without their knowledge.

102. Just as the spirit carries out its burst <note>original: "explosion." In Boehme’s theosophy, this represents the "Schrack" or the sudden, powerful flash of divine energy that transitions from darkness to light.</note> and its determined will through the teeth, and yet the teeth do not move and cannot grasp the will of the spirit; so also the holy spirit prepares, without interruption and unknown to the demon and the wrath of God, a holy seed and a temple within the house of this world.

103. But that the whole word **Spoke** <note>original: "Sprach." This is the German past tense of "to speak," referring to the Creative Word of God.</note> is compacted in the back part of the palate on the tongue, in the abyss, in the middle, within the astringent and bitter qualities <note>Boehme identifies "astringent" (harsh/sour) and "bitter" as the first two qualities of nature—forces of contraction and friction that characterize the "wrath" of God before they are enlightened by Love.</note>, and that it holds itself there; this signifies that God has compacted the place of this world, near the heart, in the middle, and has built Himself anew a house for His praise, in which He dwells with His holy angels, despite the howlings and murmurs of the demon.

104. Just as the spirit passes strongly and powerfully from the heart, through the murmurs and furies of the astringent and bitter qualities; and that without being understood by the astringent and bitter qualities, it reigns, by its burst, like a powerful king within the astringent and bitter qualities; so also the spirit of God reigns powerfully in the outermost generation <term>generation: the physical world or the final stage of "birth" where spiritual forces become manifest matter.</term> of this world, in the house of wrath, and builds there a temple incomprehensible <note>original: "incompréhensible." In 17th-century mystical contexts, this means "unable to be contained or grasped" by a lower power.</note> to the house of wrath.

105. But that the astringent and bitter spirit murmurs in this way when the spirit passes from the heart through its house, and that it dominates powerfully, this signifies that the wrath of God, together with the demons, is in opposition against the love in the

# CHAPTER NINETEEN.

---

## On the created heaven, and on the form of the earth and the water; furthermore, on light and darkness.

---

->*On the heaven.*<-

1. THE true heaven, which is for us, men, our own heaven; where the soul goes when it separates from the body; where Christ, our king, entered, and from whence he came—being begotten of his father to become man in the womb of the Virgin Mary—this heaven, I say, has been hidden until now from the children of men, and it has been the subject of a multitude of opinions.

2. Thus have scholars argued over this in a number of strange writings, and have overwhelmed each other with insults and slurs, which has caused the holy name of God to be dishonored; that his members <note>In this mystical context, "members" likely refers to the "members of Christ," meaning other believers or the community of the faithful.</note> have been wounded; that his temple has been destroyed, and that the sacred heaven has been profaned by these outrageous speeches and these enmities.

3. Everywhere, men have imagined that heaven was several hundred, or even several thousand miles from this earth; and that it was inhabited by God alone. There have also been several <term>natural philosophers</term> <note>original: "physiciens". In the 17th and 18th centuries, this referred to "natural philosophers"—those who studied the physical laws of nature and the cosmos—rather than medical doctors.</note> who have undertaken to measure this hei- <note>The word is cut off at the end of the page; it is likely "hauteur" or "height."</note>

<vocab>heaven, Christ, Virgin Mary, God, scholars, natural philosophers</vocab>

<summary>This page introduces a spiritual definition of heaven, criticizing scholars who treat it as a distant physical location and arguing that such debates have caused religious strife.</summary>
<keywords>heaven, Christ, Virgin Mary, cosmology, scholars, natural philosophy, spiritual geography</keywords>

--- Page 135 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 19. The first word "height" completes the sentence from the previous page, where the author was criticizing natural philosophers who tried to measure the distance to heaven.</meta>

HEADER: CH. 19. ( 121 )

height, and have put forward entirely ridiculous things on that subject.

4. In truth, before the time of my knowledge and the **manifestation of God** <note>A mystical term referring to a direct, revelatory experience of the divine.</note>, I myself regarded as the only true heaven that which extends in a circumference of blue light above the stars; having the opinion that there alone resided the particular being of God, and that he reigned in this world only by the *virtue* <note>In this context, "virtue" implies power or active influence.</note> of his **holy spirit**.

5. But as this drew me into several violent shocks—and this, no doubt, from the spirit who had affection for me—at the end I fell into a deep **melancholy** <note>Historically, "melancholy" was not just sadness, but a profound spiritual and philosophical distress or "dark night of the soul."</note> and into sadness, when I contemplated the great **abyss** of this world; furthermore, the sun and the stars, as well as the clouds, the rain, and the snow; and when I considered in my spirit the universal **creation** of this world.

6. For there I found in all things **good and evil**, love and wrath, as well in inanimate creatures, such as wood, stones, the earth, and the **elements** <note>Referring to the four classical elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</note>, as in man and animals.

7. Furthermore, I considered this little spark, man, and I sought what value he could have before God in comparison to this great work of heaven and earth.

8. But when I found that good and evil were in all things, in the elements and in the creatures, so that in this world the **impious** <note>The "unfree" or "wicked" who do not follow God's laws.</note> prospered just as **pious** men;

<summary>The author recounts a personal spiritual crisis where his old view of a distant God was shattered by the observation of both good and evil throughout the physical world. This realization led him into a deep melancholy as he struggled to understand man's place within the vast, dualistic creation.</summary>

<keywords>melancholy, spiritual crisis, good and evil, elements, abyss, manifestation of God, human soul, dualism</keywords>

--- Page 136 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s spiritual struggle described in Chapter 19. On the previous page, the author expressed distress over seeing the "impious" prosper; this page completes that thought and describes his subsequent mystical breakthrough.</meta>

( 122 ) CH. 19

that barbarian peoples <note>The author likely refers to non-Christian nations or those outside the "civilized" world of his time, whose success troubled his sense of divine justice.</note> possessed the best lands, and that prosperity followed them even more than it did virtuous people.

9. This made me entirely **melancholy** <note>In the 17th-century context, "melancholy" refers to a profound spiritual and psychological crisis, often called the "dark night of the soul."</note> and full of troubles; and I found no consolation in the **scriptures** which were nevertheless well known to me; added to this, the **demon** <note>A personification of doubt or evil influence.</note> certainly did not remain idle, and often whispered **pagan ideas** to me, about which I wish to remain silent here.

10. But when, in this affliction, an ardent and violent **impetuosity** <note>A forceful, sudden surge of spiritual longing or energy.</note> drew my spirit toward **God**—of whom I then had little or no knowledge—and my whole heart, my affection, all my thoughts, and all my will united in the intention of pressing without interruption for the **love and mercy of God**, and not letting go until he had blessed me—that is to say, until he had enlightened me by his **holy spirit**, so that I could understand his will and be delivered from my trouble—then the spirit made its breach.

11. But when, in my determined zeal, I fought so violently against God and against all the **gates of hell** <note>A biblical metaphor (Matthew 16:18) representing the powers of death and evil that bar the soul from divine truth.</note> (as if I had always new forces in reserve), resolved to risk my life there—which truly was beyond my power without the assistance of God's spirit—then, following some great assaults, my spirit penetrated through the gates of hell even into the **innermost generation of the divinity** <note>A technical term in mysticism referring to the internal, eternal process by which God's nature is "born" or reveals itself.</note>, and there it was embraced by **love**, as a husband embraces his dear wife.

<summary>The author describes a period of intense spiritual doubt caused by the worldly success of the wicked, which he eventually overcomes through a violent, determined "assault" of prayer that leads to a mystical union with the divine.</summary>
<keywords>melancholy, spiritual crisis, demon, holy spirit, gates of hell, divine union, Jacob Boehme, mystical experience</keywords>

--- Page 137 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded the author’s spiritual struggle where, after a period of deep melancholy and doubt regarding divine justice, he experienced a sudden "breach" or breakthrough into divine light through intense prayer and willpower.</meta>

<header>CH. 19. ( 123 )</header>

12. As for this kind of **triumph in the spirit**, I can neither write it nor speak it; it can only be imagined as if life were **generated** <term>generation: A key term for the author, referring to the "begetting" or the organic process of birth and development within the divine essence.</term> in the midst of death, and it is compared to the resurrection of the dead.

13. In this light, my spirit immediately saw through all things, and recognized in all creatures—in the plants and in the grass—what God is, and how He is, and what His **will** is. And also, in an instant, within this light, my will was carried by a great impulse to describe the being of God.

14. But as I could not immediately penetrate the deep generation of God in His **essence**, nor grasp it with my **reason**, a good twelve years passed <note>The author, Jacob Boehme, is likely referring to the period between his initial illumination in 1600 and the writing of his first book, *Aurora*, in 1612.</note> before true understanding was given to me; and it was with me as with a young tree that one plants in the earth, which at first is fresh and tender and of a pleasing appearance—especially when it promises to be of good yield—but which does not immediately bear fruit; and though it bears flowers, they nonetheless fall, and it is exposed to many cold winds, to frost, and to snow, before growing and bearing fruit.

15. This is how it was with my spirit; the first fire was only a seed, but not a permanent light. Since that time, many cold winds have fallen upon it; but the **will** was not extinguished.

16. This tree also often strove to try to bear fruit, and it showed itself with

flowers; but the flowers were cut away from the tree until this moment, when it finds itself producing its first fruit.

17. It is from this light that I received my knowledge, my will, and my impulse; therefore I wish to put my knowledge into writing according to the gift granted to me, and let God act, even if I should thereby irritate the world, the devil, and the gates of hell. I do not seek at all what God's intentions are in this. For I am too weak to recognize His plan: and although the spirit allows some of the things to come to be glimpsed in this light, nonetheless according to the outer man <note>The "outer man" refers to the physical, earthly self, as opposed to the "inner man" or the spiritual self.</note>, I am too weak to grasp them.

18. But the soulful spirit which <term>inqualifies: This is a technical term used by Jacob Boehme (derived from the German 'inqualieren') to describe the way the human spirit acts in union with, or takes on the qualities of, the divine essence.</term> with God, grasps them well. As for the animal body, it has only a glimmer of it, as when lightning flashes; for such is the state of the soul's most inward birthing, when, through the outermost generation, it carries itself into the ascension of the holy spirit, and crosses the gates of hell. But in an instant, it is enclosed once more by the most inward engendering; for God's wrath <gloss>locks: original: 'vĕrouille' (verrouille)</gloss> it immediately, and holds it prisoner in His power.

19. Then the knowledge of the outermost man is also enclosed there; and it is with him, in his generation of anxiety and pain, like a pregnant woman who goes into labor, who continually has the desire

<summary>Boehme explains that his writings come from a divine impulse and describes the fleeting nature of spiritual revelation, which flashes like lightning before being "locked" away again by the physical body and divine wrath.</summary>

->CH. 19. ( 125 )<-

to give birth; yet she cannot, and in her, the pangs accumulate without ceasing.

20. It is the same also with the <term>animal body: The physical, biological self, which in Boehme's theology is often at odds with the spiritual life.</term>; once it has tasted the sweetness of God [according to its measure], it remains ever hungrier and thirstier for it; but the demon defends himself powerfully through the power of the wrath of God; and a man on this path must be continually in a *laborious birthing*, and there is nothing but struggle and combat in his *birthing*.

21. I have not written this for my own praise, but to comfort the reader, so that in the event that he should desire to cross with me over my narrow bridge, he might not be immediately discouraged if the gates of hell and the wrath of God should present themselves to him and clash before his eyes.

22. When, walking together on this narrow bridge of the *region of the flesh*, we arrive at that *green meadow* <note>The "green meadow" is a recurring image in Boehme's work representing the peaceful, paradisiacal state of the soul after it has passed through the fire of spiritual trial.</note> where the wrath of God does not reach, then we shall rejoice perfectly in the trials we have suffered. Even if we should pass for fools in the eyes of the world, and even if the demon should dominate us through the power of the wrath of God, this must not trouble us at all; it will earn us a more beautiful decoration in the next world than if we had worn a royal crown in this one. For the time of this life is very short, and it does not even deserve to be called time.

<summary>Boehme describes the spiritual life as a painful, ongoing "birthing" process where the physical body longs for God while being attacked by spiritual forces. ->*Now, take notice.*<-

23. If you wish to consider what heaven is, where it is, or how it is; you have no need to cast your thoughts many thousands of *miles* away from here. For that space or that sky is not your heaven, and even if it were linked with your heaven, forming but one *body*, just as in truth there is only one *body* of God, nevertheless you did not become a creature in that place which is several hundred thousand *miles* beyond; but rather in the heaven of this world, which also contains within itself a depth beyond all human calculation.

24. For the true heaven is everywhere, even in the place where you are and where you walk. When your spirit reaches the innermost <term>generation: In the author's mystical system, a "generation" (German: *Geburt*) refers to the active, internal birthing process of divine forces or the development of being.</term> of God, and penetrates it through the <term>sidereal: Pertaining to the stars or astral influences that Boehme believed governed the physical and psychological world.</term> and fleshly generation, from that moment on, it is in heaven.

### *On the form of the earth.*

55. Several writers have taught that heaven and earth were created from nothing. However, I am surprised that among men of such great weight, not one has been found who could explain the true foundation, since the same God who exists now has existed from eternity.

56. Now, where there is nothing, nothing happens; all things must have a root, otherwise nothing would grow. If the <term>seven spirits of nature: In Boehme's cosmology, these are the seven primary qualities—such as contraction, friction, and light—that constitute God’s manifesting power.</term> had not existed from eternity, no angel, no heaven, and no earth would have come into being.

58. Yes, life pierces through death: the most external generation is death; the second is life, which exists in the fire of wrath and in love; the third is the holy life.

->*Warning.*<-

59. # CH. 19.

you know it. If you do not know it, keep silence, and listen.

61. He <note>Likely referring to the Word or the Divine Presence.</note> sat within the **salitter** <term>salitter: A word coined by Boehme, likely from the Latin "sal nitrum" (saltpeter), representing the divine "sap" or the crystalline, spiritual matter of the heavenly kingdom.</term> of the earth before the time of creation, when this same **salitter** was still transparent, and existed within the holy celestial generation, and was within the universal kingdom of this world. In this **salitter**, there were neither stones nor earth; instead, there was a celestial seed, which was generated from the seven source-spirits <term>source-spirits: Also called "fountain-spirits," these are the seven primary qualities or forces of God's being that drive all of creation.</term> of nature; for, within this seed, celestial fruits and forms arose, which served as a delicious nourishment for the angels.

62. But when the wrath <note>The "fire of wrath" in Boehme's system refers to the dark, consuming aspect of divine power that becomes manifest when separated from divine love.</note> was kindled, everything was struck with death. This is not to be understood as meaning that the seed is altogether dead: for how, within God, could a thing that draws its life from eternity ever die? Rather, the most external *generation* <term>generation: The process by which the divine essence manifests itself into physical or spiritual form.</term> was burned, frozen, drowned, and numbed.

63. Now, the second generation reproduces life within the most external; and the third is generated between the first and the second—that is to say, between heaven and hell, in the middle, in the fire of wrath; and the spirit traverses the fire of wrath, and generates the holy life which resides in the power of love.

64. And it is in this same *generation* that the dead who have sown a holy seed shall rise again; but those who have sown in the fire of wrath shall rise again in the fire of wrath: for the earth shall become living once more, since the divinity, in Christ, has generated it anew <note>original: "l'a engendrée de nouveau". This refers to the idea that the Incarnation began the process of restoring the corrupted material world to its original divine state.</note>.

<summary>Boehme describes the earth's original state as a transparent, divine substance called "salitter" that was corrupted by wrath but remains fundamentally linked to eternal life. # CH. 19.

->Take note.<-

The writer Moses says: God separated the light from the darkness, and called the light, *day*, and the darkness, *night*; and from the evening and the morning came the first day (*Gen. 1.*).

79. Since these *words, the evening and the morning*, are absolutely opposed to the course of philosophy and reason, it must be concluded from this that Moses is not the author of this, but that it was transmitted to him by his ancestors who counted all six days of creation in detail, and only presented and transmitted the creation of Adam to their descendants under obscure words. <note>Boehme suggests that the Genesis account is an inherited oral tradition. He often argues that ancient patriarchs understood the "Language of Nature" but passed it down in veiled or symbolic language that later generations struggled to interpret literally.</note>

80. For the evening and the morning did not exist before the time of the sun and the stars, which certainly and really were only created on the fourth day, a solid foundation for which I will demonstrate when I treat the creation of the sun and the stars. <note>Boehme is highlighting a classic cosmological paradox: how can "morning" and "evening" exist on the first day if the celestial bodies that define them were not created until the fourth?</note>

81. But *the day and the night* did exist; this is what I will explain here according to my knowledge. You may once again open the eyes of your spirit wide here, if you wish to understand me; otherwise you will remain blind.

82. Now, although this great work has remained hidden within man until this moment, finally, thanks to God, day is about to break; for the daw— <note>The text cuts off mid-sentence; "the daw—" likely refers to the "dawning" (*Aurora*), the title of this work and Boehme's metaphor for the spiritual enlightenment currently reaching humanity.</note>

<summary>Boehme addresses the apparent contradictions in the Genesis creation story, specifically the existence of morning and evening before the sun's creation. 108. When your light is extinguished, then you are in darkness; but the wrath of God is hidden in the darkness: when you awaken it, then it burns within you.

### *Of the Night. ( NIGHT. )* <note>original: "NACHT." Boehme frequently uses the physical pronunciation of German words to explain spiritual and cosmological truths.</note>

110. The word *night* <note>original: "nacht"</note> is first conceived in the heart; and the spirit is provoked by the <term>astringent quality</term> <note>original: "qualité astringente." In this mystical system, this refers to the first of the seven "source-spirits," characterized by contraction, coldness, and hardness—the foundation of matter.</note>, which however is not entirely comprehensible to the astringent quality; then it compacts itself upon the tongue; but while it is provoked in the region of the heart, the tongue closes the mouth during that time, until the spirit comes and makes itself felt upon the tongue: for the mouth opens suddenly and lets the spirit out.

111. Now, that the word is conceived in the first place in the heart, and is provoked by the astringent quality; this signifies that the Holy Spirit was conceived in the darkness upon the heart of God, in the <term>sidereal generation</term> <note>original: "engendrement sidérique." 112. But that it nevertheless passes through the dark way; this signifies that the Spirit also traverses the darkness which is still in a peaceful rest, and that it would bring them forth into the light, if only this darkness remained calm, and did not kindle the fire.

# CHAPTER TWENTY.

## Of the second day.

1. IT is written regarding the second day: and God said: let there be a firmament between the waters, and let there be a separation between the waters. Then God made a firmament, and separated the waters which were under the firmament from the waters which were above the firmament, and it was so; and God named the firmament, heaven; and from the evening and the morning was made the second day (Gen. 1: 6, 7, 8).

2. This description shows once again that Moses <note>The author attributes the Pentateuch—the first five books of the Bible—to Moses, following traditional Judeo-Christian belief.</note> is the author of this: for it is written unintelligibly and in an incomplete manner, although it nevertheless contains a very important meaning.

3. 4. ->*Now you will say:*<-

How is this so?

29. See: the water which, in the space above the earth, interacts <note>original: "inqualifie." A technical term in mystical philosophy describing how different qualities or elements operate within and through one another to produce a specific effect.</note> with the elements of air and *fire*, is the water of the sidereal generation <note>The "sidereal generation" refers to the realm of the stars and celestial influences that shape the physical world.</note>, in which the sidereal life exists; in which, particularly, the Holy Spirit moves, and through which the third and most inward generation is produced, in a manner incomprehensible to the wrath of God <note>In this school of thought, the "Wrath of God" represents the dark, fiery, or harsh foundation of nature that is hidden behind the light of grace.</note> which is within it; also, this same water appears no more to our eyes than the air does.

30. But that water, air, and fire are truly within one another in the space above the earth is something every intelligent man can see and understand.

31. For you often see the atmosphere very pure and very clear, and within a quarter of an hour it is covered with watery clouds.

32. That is to say, when there is a kindling <note>original: "enflammement." This refers to a spiritual or energetic activation of the celestial forces.</note> above in the stars, and below in the water on the earth, water is immediately generated; this would not happen if the wrath did not also exist within the sidereal generation.

33. But since everything is corrupted, the superior water, which is within the wrath of God, must come to the aid of the astringent qualities <note>The "astringent qualities" (qualités astringentes) represent the harsh, contracting, and hardening forces of nature—often associated with the element of Earth or the "Salt" principle in alchemy.</note>,

<header>CH. 20. ( 161 )</header>

### *The door of mystery.*

38. As for there being a firmament between the waters, which is called heaven, here is the understanding of it.

39. The universal depth from the moon to the earth *is*, with its operations, entirely within the wrathful and tangible generation <note>In this context, "generation" refers to the process of coming into being or manifesting within the physical and spiritual realm.</note>; for the moon is a goddess of tangible *engendering*; so too, the house of the demon, of death, and of hell, is in the region, or the circle, between the moon and the earth.

40. For there, the wrathful severity of God in the outermost generation, in the depth, is kindled and excited daily by the demons *and* all ungodly men, and by the great sins of men who, furthermore, interact <note>original: "inqualifient." ### CH. 20.

58. Now, there is the darkness in which King Lucifer, his angels, and carnal and impious men are captive (though still in their living bodies), as well as the spirits of the dead and damned.

59. This generation cannot see, hear, feel, smell, or understand God; it is a madness that King Lucifer thus produced in his pride.

60. The second generation is the sidereal <note>sidereal: From the Latin *sidereus* (star-like); in this context, it refers to the celestial or astral forces that bridge the spiritual and physical worlds.</note> (This refers to the life of the seven source-spirits <term>source-spirits: The seven fundamental qualities or "Quellgeister" that Böhme identifies as the building blocks of all reality, ranging from harsh contraction to light and sound.</term>), in which love and wrath now fight against each other; in which there exists the higher water which is a spirit of life, and in which or between which is the firmament of heaven, which is produced from the midst of the waters.

61. This generation penetrates the outer and arid generation; it pierces right through death, and gives birth to sidereal life within death—that is to say, in the hardened earth, in the water, in the flesh of animals and men, as well as in that of birds, fish, and reptiles.

62. And the demon can reach up to the half of this generation, as far as the wrath extends, and no further. Such is the extent of his dwelling, and no further; this is why the demon cannot know how the other part has a root in this generation.

68. The choleric harshness is called the cross; and the love-heaven is called patience; and the spirit that rises within it is named hope and faith, which qualifies with God <note>inqualifie: Likely a translation of the German "einquallieret," a term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the soul acting in harmony with or being permeated by a divine quality.</note>, and fights against the choleric harshness until it has conquered and triumphed (1 John 5:4).

69. It is in this that the entire doctrine of the Christian consists; whoever teaches another does not know what they teach; for their doctrine has neither base nor foundation; their heart is continually agitated and knows not what it must do.

70. For their spirit always seeks rest and finds it not, given that it is impatient and endlessly desires something new. And when it obtains it, it amuses itself as if it had discovered an unknown treasure; nonetheless, it remains in complete instability and perpetually seeks diversion.

71. You theologians, the spirit opens the door to you here. If you do not wish to pay attention, and lead your sheep not into green pastures but into arid heaths, you shall answer for it before the severe and imposing judgment of God. Therefore, reflect upon it.

72. I take heaven as my witness that I am here fulfilling the duty imposed upon me; for it is the spirit that drives me to this, so that I may be entirely bound to it and cannot be separated from it, no matter what may happen to me afterward.

73. The third *generation* <note>engendrement: Often used by Böhme to describe the perpetual process of the divine nature "giving birth" to itself or aspects of reality.</note> in the body of God, in this world, is hidden beneath the firmament of heaven, and the firmament of heaven qualifies with <note>inqualifie: This term describes a state where two things interpenetrate or share the same inner qualities and powers. It suggests a spiritual "working within."</note> it, though not entirely in a corporeal way, but creaturely—just as the angels and the souls of men do.

74. Now, this third generation is the holy and almighty heart of God, in which our king Jesus Christ sits with his *natural* body at the right hand of God, like a king and sovereign of the universal circuit or the location of this world, and who by his heart embraces and contains everything.

75. This firmament of heaven is his seat; and the source-spirits <term>sources-esprits: Translating the German 'Quellgeister,' these are the seven fundamental forces or qualities that drive all existence in Böhme's system.</term> of his *natural* body dominate within the universal circuit of this world; and they are bound with everything that exists in the region of love, in the sidereal generation <note>génération sidérique: The influence of the stars and the celestial realm on the physical and spiritual world.</note>. The other part of this world is linked with the demon.

76. You must not believe, as John *Calvin* does, that the body of Christ is not an almighty being, and does not reach further than the place where he is.

77. No, son of man, you are mistaken; and you do not well understand the divine power. Does not every man reach into the universal place or the circuit of this world through his sidereal source-spirits? For the place embraces the man; it makes but a single body; it is only the members that are distinct.

<summary>The author describes the third "generation" of God as the divine heart where Christ reigns, arguing that Christ's natural body is omnipresent through its "source-spirits." He specifically refutes Calvinist views on the localized nature of Christ's body, asserting a mystical unity between the individual and the universe.</summary>

79. 80. The *body* of Christ is no longer in a coarse [*tangibility*]; but in the divine [*tangibility*] of eternal nature, like the angels.

81. For at the resurrection our bodies will no longer have coarse flesh, nor bones, as they do now; but they will be similar to those of the angels; however, they will preserve all their configuration, their faculties, and all their activity. But nevertheless, we shall not have coarse [*tangibility*]; the organs of generation <note>organes de la génération: Reproductive organs.</note> and the intestines will have another form.

# CH. 20. ( 171 )

85. Thus, you must know that his body, by means of all the seven spirits <note>In Böhme’s cosmology, these are the seven foundational "Source-Qualities" or "Properties" that constitute all of existence, from the dark fire to the divine light.</note> that are in nature, *operated in union* <term>inqualifioit: A term used by Böhme (originally *inqualificiren*) to describe how one thing shares its qualities or works together with another in a single essence.</term> within the sidereal generation <note>The "sidereal generation" refers to the influences of the stars and the astral world on the human form.</note> in the region of love, and held sin, death, and the demon prisoner in the region of wrath.

87. You see likewise how heaven is also within a holy man. In whatever place you may be, whether you go or stay, if only your spirit *operates in union* <term>inqualifie: Works in harmony with or shares the same essential nature.</term> with God, from that moment you are in heaven as to the spirit, and your soul is in God. This is why Christ also says: *my sheep are in my hand, and no one shall take them from me* (John 10 <note>original: "mes brebis sont dans ma main, et personne ne me les enlèvera" — a translation of John 10:28.</note>).

88. Similarly, you see how, according to the wrath, you are perpetually in hell with all the demons. If only your eyes were opened <note>original: "s'ouvroient" — the author refers to the opening of "spiritual sight" or the "Third Eye."</note>, you would see wonders; but you are between heaven and hell, and you can see neither, and you walk upon a very narrow bridge.

<summary>Böhme explains that heaven and hell are not distant locations but internal spiritual states that exist simultaneously within the soul. He describes Christ’s resurrected body as having the power to operate across these different "regions" of love and wrath.</summary>

# CHAPTER TWENTY-ONE.

## *Of the third day.*

1. Although in the writings of Moses the spirit has kept the deepest secrets hidden within the letter, nevertheless everything is described there so regularly that, regarding the order that reigns within, there is nothing more to be desired.
2. For when God created the heaven and the earth by the word, and separated the light from the darkness, and gave each thing its seat; at that moment each thing began its generation and its qualification <note>original: "qualification" — in this context, it refers to the internal spiritual property, activity, or "source-spirit" that defines a thing's nature.</note> or operation.
3. On the first day, God compacted or created by the force of the spirit the corrupt *salitter* <note>A term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the divine "salt-nitre" or the fundamental substance of the universe, which here is described as having become "corrupt" following the rebellion of the angels.</note>, which came from the ignition of his wrath; for the word *schuff* <note>original German: "schuf," meaning "created." The author provides a mystical etymology here, suggesting that the act of creation was a process of densification or "coagulation."</note> signifies a coagulation.
4. Through this coagulation of the corrupt, wrathful *salitter*, King *Lucifer*, along with his angels, was pushed back like a powerless prince into the abyss of the *salitter* of wrath—into that place where the external [*graspability*] <note>original: "saisissabilité" — the quality of being tangible, material, or perceptible to the senses.</note>, half-dead, is generated; this is the space between the dead earth and the goddess of nature or the *moon*.
5. When this was done, the space became clear; the light, as well as the hidden heaven, separated from the darkness; and the globe of the earth was drawn once in rotation in the great wheel

### CH. 21. ( 177 )

For Moses writes thus: and God said: let the earth produce plants and grass, which generate their seed, and fruit trees which bear fruit each after its kind, and each having its seed in itself upon the earth; and it was so; and the earth produced plants and grasses which generate their seed each according to its kind, and trees which bore fruit, and which had in them their own seed, each according to its kind; and God saw that it was good; and from the evening and from the morning came the third day (Genesis 1:11, 12, 13).

10. This is written very correctly and very exactly; but the true foundation is hidden within the words, and has never been understood by man. For since the time of the Fall, man has never been able to understand the **internal generation**, and what the *heavenly engendering* <note>The author distinguishes between the physical growth we see and the spiritual "birthing" process occurring within the divine essence.</note> is like; rather, his reason has remained enclosed within the [ **graspability** <note>original: "saisissabilité" — referring to the material world that can be perceived and "grasped" by the physical senses.</note> ] of the exterior, and has not been able to pierce through heaven to contemplate the divine internal generation which is also everywhere within the corrupted earth.

11. One must not think here that God has made something new, which did not exist before; for if that were so, there would have been another God, which is impossible.

12. For outside of this one God, there is nothing, seeing as even the gates of hell are not outside of this one God. There has only been a separation between the love in the light, and between the wrath kindled in the darkness; thus one cannot reach the other, and they are—

**23.** Likewise, the earth produced no other foreign life than that which has been eternally in her. **30.** Behold. **31.** And he did not remain in the region of love; but he retrograded from love into wrath, and allowed himself to be filled with desire for his dead mother <note>The "dead mother" refers to the corrupted, material earth from which man’s physical body was formed.</note>, in order to nourish himself from her, to suckle at her breast, and to live upon her stem.

<header>CH. 21. ( 183 )</header>

**32.** Now, what he sought has happened to him; by his most external **generation** <note>original: "engendrement"</note>, he has brought himself into the death of his mother <note>The "mother" refers to the "matrix" or the material nature of the earth from which the physical body is formed.</note>, and through his life he has moved from love into the region of the **wrathful-sidereal generation** <note>The "wrathful-sidereal" refers to the realm of the stars and physical elements where the "wrath" (the harsh, dark fire of nature) is active, as opposed to the pure light of the divine realm.</note>.

**33.** Now he is there between heaven and hell, in the sight of the demon, in his kingdom. The demon fights and struggles perpetually against him, either to cast him back from his country onto the earth, or to make of him a son of wrath in hell.

->*What is now his hope?*<-

**34.** Reflect, you blind pagans; reflect, you corrupters and falsifiers of the scriptures; open your eyes, and do not disdain this simplicity: for God is hidden in the **center** <note>In Böhme’s mysticism, the "center" is the internal point of origin where the divine spark resides within the heart of all things.</note>, and he is even much more simple, but you do not perceive him.

**36.** But you must know that the apple on its stem **inqualifies** <term>Inqualifies: To permeate or intermingle divine qualities with another substance; here, the fruit shares the same spiritual essence as the Word.</term> equally by its innermost generation with the word of God, by the power of which it has pushed out from the earth. Now,

### The open gate of the earth.

->*Now someone might ask:*<-

From what matter or from what power have the grass, the plants, and the trees then proceeded? What then is their essence, and what constitutes these creatures?

58. The simple man <note>original: "le simple"; referring to the common person or the theologically uneducated who lacks spiritual insight into the mysteries of nature.</note> says that God made everything out of nothing; but he does not know this same God, and he does not know what He is. When he considers the earth, as well as the depth that is above the earth, he then thinks that this is not God, or that God is not there. He always imagines that God only dwells above the blue sky, or the stars, and that He reigns in this world by a spirit that has gone forth from Him; that His body, or the unity of His action, is not present on the earth or in the earth.

59. This is also an opinion that I have read in books and in the writings of the doctors <note>original: "docteurs"; a term for learned theologians or established scholars of the Church.</note>; and indeed, a great many disputes and controversies have arisen on this point among the learned.

60. But as God in His great love has opened for me the gate of His being, and as He remembers the covenant He has made with man,

For the being of God is like a wheel, where several wheels show themselves one within the other, above, below, and on the side, and turn continually one within the other. One indeed sees the wheel there, and is filled with great wonder; however, one cannot, in its revolution <term>revolution: the circular motion or turning of the divine "wheel" of creation</term>, learn everything all at once, or fully conceive of it. But the more one contemplates the wheel, the more one learns of its form; and the more one learns, the more one feels drawn to the wheel: for one always sees something more and more wonderful, so much so that man never tires of looking and learning.

62. Also, what I have not been able to sufficiently explain in one place regarding these great mysteries, you will find in another; and what I cannot write in this book, given the greatness of the subject and my own insufficiency, you will find in the books that follow.

### *Of the seven spirits of God, and of their operation in the earth.*

73. First, there is the **astringent spirit** <note>original: "esprit astringent"; the first of the seven "source-spirits" in Böhme's system, representing the force of contraction, cold, and hardening</note>, which, in the sidereal <note>meaning "of the stars"; the cosmic influence on earthly matter</note> generation of the seven source-spirits, gathers a mass together in the earth through the ignition of the higher generation above the earth, and dries this mass by its sharp cold. Just as it congeals water and turns it into ice, so it also congeals the water within the earth and turns it into a dry mass.

74. Next, the **bitter spirit** <note>original: "esprit amer"; the second source-spirit, representing movement, friction, and the sting of life</note> which exists in the fire-flash <note>original: "l'éclair de feu"; the moment of spiritual or physical ignition</note> is also in the materials or in the mass. It cannot bear to be held prisoner in that which is dried; instead, it rubs against the astringent spirit within the dried mass until the fire is ignited. Now, when this happens, the bitter spirit leaps up and conquers its life.

->Understand this precisely.<-

75. Aside from plants and metals, you can perceive or find nothing in the earth but astringency, bitterness, and water. But nevertheless, the water within is sweet and entirely opposed to the other two.

76. Furthermore, it is fluid; and the other two are compact, harsh, and bitter. One is always set against the other, and for this reason there are perpetually assaults, attacks, and

battles; now, in the state of war of these three things, life does not yet exist; instead, they are a valley of darkness; they are three things that can never tolerate one another, and among which there is a perpetual struggle.

77. It is from there that mobility takes its source; the wrath of God <note>original: "la colère de Dieu"; in Böhme’s thought, the "wrath" is the dark, fire-principle of God that becomes a destructive force when separated from His light and love</note>, which is hidden in rest, likewise draws its origin from there. Such is also the origin of the demon <note>referring to Lucifer</note>, of death and of hell, as you can inform yourself on this in the place where the fall of the demon is discussed.

### *The depth in the center of the generation <note>original: "l’engendrement"; the process by which spiritual qualities manifest into physical form</note>.*

78. When, therefore, these three things—astringency, bitterness, and sweetness—clash thus with one another, the astringent quality <note>the contracting, cooling force</note> holds the first rank, since it is the strongest, and it powerfully compresses the sweet quality; for the sweet quality is mild and expansive because of its flexibility, and it must surrender itself as a prisoner.

79. Now, when this happens, from then on the bitter quality <note>the stinging, mobile force</note> is imprisoned along with it in the body of the sweet water, and is also dried out at the same time; then astringency, sweetness, and bitterness are within one another, and deliver strong attacks within the dried mass, until this mass is arid, for the astringent quality always tightens and dries more and more.

80. But when the sweet water can no longer

81. And in this rising of anguish, an agonizing moisture is engendered. From this results a sweat that breaks through, such as one sees breaking through a dying man; and this same sweat inter-qualifies <term>inqualifie: a technical term in this philosophy meaning that forces or qualities permeate one another and share their properties</term> with the astringent and bitter qualities: for it is their child that they have fathered from the sweet water, after having killed this water and put it to death.

82. 83. But the body that was first squeezed out of the sweet water remains dead; and the sweat of the body, which inter-qualifies with the astringent and bitter qualities, takes up its dwelling there and spreads itself, and becomes strong, fat, and gluttonous.

84. Now therefore, the two qualities, astringent and bitter, cannot abandon their dispute and their opposition; instead, they continually fight one another. The astringent quality is strong, and the bitter quality is agile.

85. Thus, when the astringent quality attacks the bitter quality, the latter throws itself to the side and carries away the sap <note>original: "suc"; referring to the vital fluid or life-essence of the plant</note> of the child; then the astringent quality presses it tightly from all sides, seeking to take it prisoner; this causes the bitter quality to strive to launch itself out of the body, extending itself as far as it can.

86. 87. Such then is the growth, the conglomeration, or the incorporation of a root, just as it occurs in the earth.

90. 92. 93. It must not be imagined that the water dies altogether. No, that cannot be; but the astringent spirit takes prisoner, in its cold fire, the sweetness or the oily portion of the water; it *works within* it and employs it for itself.

### CH. 21. ( 199 )

94. For its spirit is completely numbed in death; then it employs water to revive itself, and it removes all its fat <note>original: "graisse"; referring to the oily essence or nourishing "unctuosity" of the soul-spirit.</note>, and takes it into its power.

95. Thus, there arises from the water an anxious sweat, which stands between death and life; and the fire of heat cannot ignite: for the unctuosity <term>unctuosity: the nourishing, oily essence within the "sweet water" that serves as the fuel for the divine light.</term> is a prisoner in the cold fire <note>original: "feu froid"; in Böhme's thought, this represents the harsh, contractive energy of the First Principle which has power but no light.</note>, and the entire body remains a valley of darkness; it exists in an anxious birth <note>original: "enfantement angoisseux"; Böhme's term for the agonizing struggle of nature as it tries to break through the darkness to reach the light.</note>, and cannot reach life.

96. For the life which is in the light cannot rise up in the hard, bitter, and astringent <note>The "astringent" quality is the force of contraction and cold that gives things form but can also lead to hardness and spiritual death.</note> body, seeing that it is a prisoner in the cold fire; but it is not entirely dead.

97. Now, you see that all of this is true. Take a root which is of a warm quality, and put it in warm water; or else take it in your mouth and make it warm and moist, and you will soon see how its life will act and operate. But as long as it is deprived of heat, it is a prisoner in death, and is as cold as any other root or any other piece of wood.

98. Thus you see clearly that, as for the root, the body also is dead: for if the force is taken from the root, from that moment the body is a dead carcass and can perform nothing; and the reason is that the astringent and bitter spirit has killed the body of the water, and has drawn its unctuosity to itself, and has sucked its spirit into the dead body.

100. From there, they would receive their true life. They would be satisfied with the light, and would rejoice greatly in it. 101. 102. The first three, namely, the astringent, the sweet, and the bitter, belong to the configuration of the body, and in them exist mobility and corporeality. 103. ### CH. 21.

...must rise through the astringent and bitter death, and bring forth a body in the half-dead water; then this same body is at once good and evil, dead and living: for it must immediately draw to itself the sap of fury <note>original: "suc de la fureur"; in Böhme's system, this "fury" or "wrath" is the harsh, dark quality of nature that provides the energy for existence but must be overcome by light.</note> and the body of death, and remain in a corporization <term>corporization: the process of taking on a physical or "bodily" form from spiritual qualities.</term> and a power analogous to what the earth is, which is the mother.

110. But how life remains hidden under and within the earth is what I wish to demonstrate to you.

111. Look. A man becomes unwell and sick, and if remedies are not brought to him, he falls into death, whether his illness came from a bitter and astringent <term>astringent: the first "source-spirit" in Böhme’s system, characterized by contraction, coldness, and hardness.</term> plant growing from the earth, or from a harmful and deadly water, or from various mixtures of herbs of the earth, as well as from bad meats and the discouragement <note>original: "découragemens"; in the medical language of the time, this refers to a physical sinking or weakening of the "vital spirits" that sustain life.</note> that results from them.

112. ### The depth in the circle of generation.

->Now someone might ask:<-

What then is the substance and the manner of being of the earth's sidereal generation <term>sidereal generation: the cosmic process of formation influenced by the stars and the astral plane, which Böhme sees as an intermediate level between spirit and matter.</term>, such that it began to in-qualify <term>in-qualify: (French: *inqualifier*) to permeate or influence a substance with specific spiritual or natural qualities.</term> and produce on a certain day, earlier than the sidereal generation in the Deep <note>original: "profondeur"; in Böhme’s cosmology, this refers to the "Great Deep" or the vast, subtle expanse of the heavens/aether.</note> or the space above the earth? This is asked because the fire in the Deep above the earth is more refined and more flammable than that which is in the earth; and also since the earth needs to be heated by the fire in the Deep above the earth so that it might be able to bring forth fruits.

118. Look, you, intelligent spirit; it is with you that the spirit speaks, and not with the dead spirit of the flesh; open wide the gates of your sidereal birth; raise that part of your sidereal generation which is in the light; let the other remain in the wrath <note>original: "colère"; Böhme's "Wrath" or *Grimmigkeit* refers to the harsh, dark, and consuming fire of nature that must be overcome by light.</note>; and take care that your soulful generation <note>original: "génération animique"; the process of the soul's formation and its interaction with spiritual qualities.</note> in-qualifies entirely with the light.

119. And when you have placed yourself in this state, you will then be such as the heaven and the earth are, or such as the universal divinity is through its births in this world. If you have not thus disposed yourself, you are still blind, even when...

121. The painter and the one who believed him would both be in error. Both would see there nothing but wood and colors. It would be a blind man leading another. ### CH. 21.

124. For the first source-spirit is the *astringent*, cold, harsh, and hard *generation*; and it belongs to the first day. 125. For it was there that the harsh earth and the hard, compact stones appeared; that they were congealed together; that, furthermore, the firm firmament of heaven was generated, and that the seven spirits of God remained hidden within the hard harshness.

126. <header>CH. 21. ( 207 )</header>

...remained in its harsh seat, like a **compacted death**, and that which was sweet transpired by the power of the light, through that which was compact.

131. The third day is most justly attributed to **Mars**, since he is a bitter, furious, and restless spirit. In the third revolution of the earth, the <term>bitter quality: The "gall" or "stinging" force (called *Bitter* by Böhme), representing the second source-spirit that provides movement and friction.</term> and the astringent quality reacted upon each other.

->*Understand this profound thing precisely.*<-

132. When the light had penetrated into the sweet water, through the astringent spirit, the flash of fire or the explosion of light, igniting in the water, rose into the astringent, hard, and dead qualities, and made everything agile; it is from this that **mobility** came.

123. The movement of the three <term>engenderings: Also translated as "generations," referring to the internal processes of divine birth.</term> lasted six days and six nights, during which the seven spirits of God were in full activity of generation, as was the heart of the spirits; and during this time the *salitter* of the earth made six revolutions in the great wheel; which wheel is the seven <term>source-spirits: The seven primary qualities or forces of God and nature (e.g., the astringent, the bitter, the sweet, etc.).</term> of God; and at each revolution, there was a particular work engendered according to the *actual* degree of the source-spirits.

124. For the first source-spirit is the astringent, cold, harsh, and hard *engendering*; and it belongs to the first day. In the <term>sidereal generation: The formation of the stars and the celestial influences.</term>, astrologers call the *engendering* that was brought forth on the first day "Saturnine."

125. For it was then that the harsh earth and the hard, compact stones appeared; that they were conglomerated together; and that, furthermore, the firm firmament of heaven was engendered, and that the seven spirits of God remained hidden within that hard harshness.

126. Astrologers apply the second day to the sun; but it belongs to Jupiter, to use astrological language: for, on the second day, the light burst from the heart of the seven source-spirits through the hard quality of heaven, and produced a softening in the compact water of heaven, and the light became brilliant within this softening.

128. 129. The harsh firmament is the astringent quality, and the sweet firmament is the water, in which rises the light of life, which is the brightness of the Son of God.

130. And it is in this manner that knowledge and the light of life rise in man; it is also in this manner that the light of God is formed, generated, and rises in this world.

131. ### ( 208 ) CH. 21.

133. Now, I am not speaking here only of the sky above the earth; rather, this movement and this <term>engendering: The process of bringing something into being; a central theme in Böhme’s view of nature as a living, reproductive organism.</term> have taken place equally within the earth and everywhere.

134. But just as before the time of wrath <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "time of wrath" refers to the disruption of cosmic harmony (often linked to the fall of Lucifer), which caused the qualities of nature to become harsh and conflicting.</note> the celestial fruits originated from this movement of the <term>source-spirits: The seven fundamental forces or "Quellgeister" that drive all of existence, such as the astringent, the sweet, and the bitter.</term>, and just as it was by their movement that they passed and were altered, so it was on the third day of the generation of creation that they rose up through the movement of the flash of fire into the <term>astringent quality: The first source-spirit, which acts as a contracting force that hardens and fixes matter.</term> of the earth.

135. Although the universal divinity is now hidden in the center of the earth, the earth would not have been able, for all that, to engender celestial fruits; for the astringent spirit has drawn the harsh bolt of death <note>The "bolt of death" represents the hardening of the physical world, which traps the divine light and prevents the earth from naturally producing the perfect, spiritual fruits of the heavenly realm.</note>; so that, by this, the heart of the divinity, in all its engendering, has remained hidden in its soft and luminous heaven.

136. For the outermost generation is nature, and it is not fitting for *this nature* to carry itself backward into the heart of God; nor is it able to do so: but it is the body in which the source-spirits are engendered, and they manifest their birth through their fruits.

137. This is why the earth began to vegetate on the third day, when the source-spirits were in the explosion of the word, or in the fire of the lightning flash <note>Original: "l'éclair." This "flash" is the moment of transition where the dark, cold qualities of nature are ignited by light, resulting in life and consciousness.</note>.
