## CHAPTER TWENTY-SECOND.

### Of the birth of the stars, and of the creation of the fourth day.

1. Here begins the description of the <term>sidereal generation: The process by which the stars and heavenly bodies were brought into being from the divine forces of nature.</term>, and it is an essential thing to note the significance of the first title of this book, which is titled: *The Rising Dawn* <note>original: "l'Aurore Naissante," the French translation of Böhme's famous work *Aurora*. The title symbolizes the beginning of spiritual enlightenment.</note>. For here, the most simple man will be able to see and understand the being of God just as well.

2. Let the reader only not sink into their own unbelief, and into their <term>dense conception: A state of mind limited by material thinking, unable to perceive the spiritual essence within nature.</term>; for I have, as testimony and as witnesses, all of nature with all its children. If you are reasonable, look around you; look at yourself, and consider yourself exactly, then you will soon find by what kind of spirit I write.

3. For my part, I wish to fulfill the orders of the spirit with obedience. Pay attention to yourselves, then, and do not let yourselves be locked in while the way is free; for here the gate of knowledge is open.

4. And although the spirit walks in a direction contrary to that of some <term>astrologers: In this context, Böhme refers to those who study the stars only through outward calculations rather than through an understanding of the divine spirit that formed them.</term>, that does not stop me at all; I must rather obey God than men; they are blind in the spirit; if they do not wish to see, they are masters of remaining in their blindness.

5. When on the third day the flash of fire sprang from the <term>sweet water: In Böhme’s cosmology, this is the "light-water," a spiritual essence that provides the substance for life and growth.</term> that shone therein (which flash is the <term>bitter quality: A fundamental force of nature characterized by tension and movement, arising from the interaction of light and fire.</term> that is generated from the fiery explosion of fire within the water);

6. Then the entire nature of this world was bubbling and in motion, both within the earth and above the earth; and life began to be generated anew in all things.

7. From the earth rose grass, plants, and trees; and within the earth rose silver, gold, and every kind of mineral; and in the depths above the earth, there arose the marvelous formation of the powers.

8. But so that you may understand what the substance, composition, and generation of all these things are, I wish to describe them all one after another, each in its proper rank, so that you may grasp the basis of this great mystery: and 1st, I shall begin by speaking of the earth; 2nd, then of the space above the earth; 3rd, of the conglomeration of the body of the stars; 4th, of the <term>seven principal qualities of the planets: The seven primary forces or "spirits" that Böhme believes govern both the cosmos and the human soul.</term> and of their heart, which is the sun; 5th, of the four elements; 6th, of the external and perceptible generation that results from this entire regime or government; and 7th, of the admirable proportions and the virtuality of the <term>universal wheel of nature: The cyclical, interconnected system of divine forces that drives all growth and change in the universe.</term>

9. I now summon before this mirror <note>The "mirror" here refers to the book itself or the spiritual vision the author is presenting to the reader.</note> all the friends of the estimable and holy arts of philosophy...

11. Thus my intention would be to proceed in accordance with these rules.

13. For I must say that their rules are my masters, and it is from them that I hold the first elements of my knowledge. This is why I have no design to destroy or reform them, especially since I could not, having not learned them; but I leave them in their place.

20. Then I would remain on the ground, I would be trampled underfoot, whereas before I appeared with glory at a beautiful door. For what would this bar serve then? it would be nothing more than a piece of wood, made to be thrown into the fire.

21. Look. I tell you a secret. 28. One must believe, indeed, that before and after the flood, the principal instruction and the doctrine of the patriarchs aimed to turn the eyes of men toward the creation, as one sees that the entire book of Job aims toward that.

31. They had indeed honored and adored the sun and the stars as Gods; but they had not recognized how these celestial bodies had been created, nor from where they originated.

32. However, they could have thought that these celestial bodies originated from something, and that this "something" which had created them was greater and older than the stars.

33. Furthermore, they had the earth and stones as examples, which must have originated from something, just as men and all creatures of the earth did. All this was a testimony to them that in all these things there was a power even more mighty which had created them.

34. But why should I write so much on the blindness of the pagans? Are our <term>doctors<note>original French: "docteurs." They know well in truth that there is a God who created all things, but they do not know where this same God is, nor how He is.

35. If they want to write of God, they seek Him outside of this world, and in a single heaven only, as if He were an image that one compared to something. They grant indeed that this same God governs everything in this world by a spirit, but they want His bodily dwelling to be only in a heaven beyond several thousands of miles.

36. Come here, <term>doctors<note>original French: "docteurs." In this context, the author refers to the established religious scholars and theologians of his time, whom he often accuses of having "book learning" without spiritual insight.</note>>; if you are in the truth, answer the spirit; I have some questions to ask you.

37. What do you think existed in the place of this world before the time of this world? Or rather, from where do you think the earth and the stars originated? Or else, what do you think exists in the depth above the earth, and from where did this space originate? Or rather, what do you think man is—the image of God—in whom God dwells? Or else, what do you think the <term>wrath of God<note>original French: "colère de Dieu." A central concept in this type of mystical philosophy, often referring to a specific "quality" or "source" within the divine nature that becomes a consuming fire when separated from God's light.</note>> is? Or what is it in man that displeases God, so that He torments him—since it is He who created him—or why should He regard him as guilty and condemn him to eternal punishment?

38. Why then did He create that in which man goes astray? Consequently, must this thing not be even much worse? Why, and from where did it originate? Or else, what is the cause, or the beginning, or the <term>generation<note>original French: "génération." Used here in the sense of the "birthing" or "originating process" of a spiritual quality.</note>> of the fiery wrath of God, from which hell and the demon originated? Or else, from where does it come that all creatures in this world devour, strike, and fight one another, and yet sin is attributed only to man alone?

39. Or rather, from where did the venomous and wicked animals originate, and the worms as well as all the insects? Or else, from where did the holy angels originate? And finally, what is the soul of man and the great God Himself?

40. Here, give a just and solid answer; justify it, and abstain from your disputes. If you can testify through your external writings that you know the true unique God; how He is in love and in <term>wrath<note>original French: "colère." In this mystical context, wrath is not just an emotion but a fundamental, consuming quality of the divine nature that exists in tension with His love.</note>>, and what He is; and if you can justify that God is not in the stars, the <term>elements<note>original French: "élémens." Refers to the four traditional elements (earth, air, fire, water) which were believed to compose all physical matter.</note>>, the earth, the stones, men, animals, worms, leaves, plants, grass, heaven and earth, and that this whole is not God Himself, and that my spirit is in error; then I am willing to be the first to burn my book, to disavow and to curse all that I have written, and to submit myself with docility to your instructions.

41. However, this is not to say that I cannot err at all. For there are certain things that are not sufficiently clarified, and which seem written as if from a simple glimpse of the great God, when the <term>wheel of nature<note>original French: "roue de la nature." A signature concept in theosophy, representing the cyclical and unfolding processes of divine manifestation in the physical world.</note>> turns too rapidly; and man, with his thick and half-dead intelligence, cannot grasp them sufficiently.

42. But what you do not find clarified and developed in one place, you will find in another; if it is not in this book, it will be in the following ones. Now you will say that it is not proper for me to ask such questions. For the divinity is a <term>mystery<note>original French: "mystère." Used here to describe the "unfathomable" nature of God that traditional theologians argued should not be investigated or "scrutinized" by common people.</note>> that no one can search into.

43. Listen. If it is not proper for me to question, it is not proper for you to judge me either.

<vocab>God, love, wrath, stars, elements, nature, intelligence, mystery, to search/scrutinize</vocab>

<summary>The author challenges the established clergy to prove that God is not present in all of creation, offering to burn his own work if they can, while also admitting the difficulty of perfectly capturing divine truths through limited human intellect.</summary>

### CH. 22.

But if you boast of knowing the light and being the guides of the blind, and you yourselves are blind, how then can you show the way to the blind? Do you not run the risk, in your blindness, of falling, both one and the other?

But, you will say to me: We are not blind and we see the path of light well.

Why then do you dispute concerning the way of light which, nevertheless, according to you, no one sees clearly?

44. You teach the way to others, and yet you are always seeking it yourselves. You grope in the <term>darkness<note>original French: "ténèbres." In mystical literature, this refers to the lack of spiritual illumination or the state of the soul before it is "reborn" in the light of God.</note>>, and you do not see it; or rather, would you suppose it to be a sin for someone to inquire what the way is?

45. Oh! you blind men, abstain from disputes, do not shed innocent blood, do not ravage countries and states on this account according to the will and intentions of the <term>demon<note>original French: "démon." Used here to represent the adversarial spirit that incites division, pride, and war, contrasting with the "spirit of God."</note>>; but clothe yourselves in the garment of peace; fill yourselves with love for one another, and practice gentleness. Abstain from pride and greed. Let no one feel envy toward another. Do not let yourselves be inflamed by the fire of anger; but live in amenity, in chastity, in friendship, and in purity; then you will be, and you will all live in God.

46. For you have no need to say: where is God? Listen, you blind men, you live in God, and God is in you; and if

...you live holily, from that moment you are yourself God. Wherever you cast your gaze, there is God.

47. When you contemplate the space between the stars and the earth, will you say, "that is not God," or rather, "God is not there"? O you, miserable and corrupt man, let yourself be taught. For in this depth above the earth—where you see and recognize nothing, and where you say there is nothing—there nonetheless is the God of holy light, God in his trinity; and he is <term>generating<note>original French: "engendrant." In this mystical context, it refers to the continuous process of divine self-manifestation or the "birth" of God's qualities within the universe.</note>> there just as in the higher heaven above this world.

48. Or do you suppose that at the time of the creation of this world, he separated himself from his throne where he has sat from all eternity? Oh, no! That cannot be, and even if he himself willed it, he could not, for he himself is everything. Just as a limb cannot separate itself from the body; likewise, one cannot <term>cut God off<note>original French: "retrancher Dieu." The author is arguing for the omnipresence of the divine; God cannot be "subtracted" from any part of existence because existence itself is part of His "body."</note>> from anything whatsoever.

49. But that there should be in him so great a number of *principles of formation*, this comes from his eternal generation which is primarily <term>trinary<note>original French: "trinaire." This refers to the Trinity (Father, Son, Holy Spirit) acting as the fundamental structure of all creation.</note>>, and which produces itself from this same trinary in an infinite and immeasurable way.

50. I will write here concerning this same generation, and I will show the children of the final world what God is; not out of boastfulness or pride, nor to insult or despise anyone at all; no. The spirit will instruct you gently and amicably, as a father teaches <insert>his children</insert>...

<summary>The author describes God's omnipresence, arguing that the divine is not distant from the world but active in every part of the cosmos through an eternal "trinary" process of generation. He maintains that God is inseparable from creation, much like a body is inseparable from its limbs.</summary>

But in the Spirit of God, my spirit, birthed within, penetrates everywhere; not, in truth, in a permanent manner; but when the spirit of God's love penetrates my spirit, then the <term>soulish generation<note>original French: "génération animique." ...better than the others? See, O blind man, wherever love and gentleness arise, there the heart of God arises. For the heart of God is birthed in the sweet water of the kindled light, whether within man or outside of man. It is brought forth everywhere in the center, in the middle, between the outermost generation and the innermost generation. <note>In this mystical philosophy, "generation" refers to the continuous process of spiritual or natural birth—the way the divine essence manifests through different layers of reality.</note>

55. Whatever you may look upon, God is there; but <term>graspability<note>original French: "saisissabilité." This refers to the state of being tangible or perceptible to the senses and the fallen intellect.</note>> exists in this world within the wrath that the devil has kindled; and the light, or the heart of God, is birthed in the hidden core in the midst of the wrath; it is ungraspable to the wrath, and each remains in its own seat. <note>The "wrath" (colère) refers to a harsh, dark quality of nature that exists in tension with God's light; the author suggests they occupy the same space but do not mix.</note>

56. I do not thereby approve of the unbelief of the Jews, the Turks, and the Pagans, nor their stubbornness, their fury, and their animosity against Christians. No; these are true snares of the Devil who, through them, draws men toward pride, greed, envy, and wrath, and kindles within them the infernal fire. I would be no more justified in saying that these four sons of the devil do not also reign within Christendom, and even within every individual man.

->But you will say:<-

What then is the difference between Christians, Jews, Turks, and Pagans?

Here the spirit opens the doors; if you do not see, you must be blind.

57. Such is first of all the distinction that God has maintained everywhere: namely, that all those who know what God is and how they must serve him may, through their knowledge, pierce through the **wrath** and into the **love of God**, and subdue the demon. If they do not do so, they are no better than those who do not know God. <note>The "wrath" (colère) in this mystical tradition refers to the harsh, fiery quality of existence that must be overcome by "love" to reach the divine presence.</note>

58. But he who, without knowing the way, pierces through the wrath into the love, is similar to the one who has pierced through to that point by means of his knowledge. As for those who persevere in the wrath, and kindle it entirely within themselves, they shall all be gathered together, whether they be Christians, Jews, Turks, or Pagans. <note>In the 17th and 18th centuries, the term "Turk" was commonly used by European writers to refer to all Muslims.</note>

59. Or else, what do you think of the means by which one can serve God? Do you wish to feign with him, or disguise your <term>generation<note>original French: "engendrement." This refers to the spiritual birthing or the fundamental nature of one's inner being, which the author argues cannot be faked through outward ritual.</note>>?

60. I am persuaded that you are a beautiful angel; but he who has love in his heart; who leads a life of mercy and gentleness; who fights against wickedness, and penetrates through death into the light; it is that person who lives in God, and is one single spirit with God.

61. For God has no need of other services, except that his creation—which is in his body, or in his divine circumscription—does not distance itself from him, but rather that it be holy, as he is holy. <note>The "divine circumscription" implies the boundaries or the totality of the divine presence within which all things exist.</note>

62. It is for this reason also that God gave the law to the Jews, so that they might move with zeal toward holiness and toward love, so that the world...

73. But as the water, which is the spirit of the sidereal life, existed in the midst of the wrath and also in death, each body was also formed according to what the birth was destined to produce: life and mobility.

### The depth.

->*Understand this well.*<-

75. When, on the third day, the <term>flash of fire<note>original: "éclair de feu." Also known in Boehme's original German as the "Blitz" or "Schrack," this is the sudden ignition of life and light within the dark, cold elements of "death."</note>> ignited in the water of death, life then pierced through the dead body of the water and the earth.

76. Now, the dead water and the earth no longer grasp anything but the flash or the terrifying explosion of fire through which their mobility exists. But as for the light that rises quite gently within the flash of fire, neither the earth nor the dead water can grasp it.

77. It keeps its seat in the kernel, which is the <term>unctuosity<note>original: "onctuosité." A technical term in 17th-century alchemy and theosophy referring to a spiritual "oil" or "sap" that serves as the fuel for the divine light, allowing it to burn gently and provide life.</note>>, or the water of life, or indeed heaven. For it is the body of life that death cannot reach, and which nevertheless rises up within death. Thus, wrath cannot reach it; instead, wrath remains in the explosion of the flash of fire and produces mobility in the dead body of the earth and in the water.

78. Nevertheless, the light penetrates it quite gently and forms the *generation* <note>original: "l’engendrement." Refers to the organic growth or manifestation of living things.</note> which, through the explosion of the flash of fire, has acquired its conglomerated body.

->*Of metals within the earth.*<-

84. Metals have the same substance and the same generation as the plants that are upon the earth. For at the time of the ignition of wrath <note>original: "l'enflammement de la colère." In Boehme’s cosmology, "wrath" is the harsh, contracting quality of nature that provides the foundation for life but must be balanced by light/love.</note>, in the current degree of the wheel where the seventh spirit of nature was found, metal and mineral were in the operation of love, there where sweet benignity is generated, behind the flash of fire. It is there that the holy heaven exists, which, in this generation—when love holds the first rank—manifests itself in a holy clarity, in beautiful colors, and resembling gold, silver, and precious stones.

85. But silver and gold in dead [ tangibility <note>original: "saisissabilité." A key term in this translation referring to the state where spiritual, "un-graspable" reality becomes physical, "graspable" matter.</note> ] are but a dark stone in comparison to the root of the celestial generation. I only cast this here so that you may know what its origin is.

86. Now, as gold was the most beautiful ascension and the most beautiful generation in the holy and celestial nature, it is also cherished by men above all things in this world. For nature has well written in the heart of man that this gold was more beautiful than other stones and the earth, but she was not able to manifest to him the base from which it proceeds, nor from whence it came; upon which you may now pay attention to the dawn of the day <note>original: "l’aurore du jour." Likely an allusion to the title of this work, *Aurora*, signifying the dawning of spiritual understanding.</note>.

...according to how the <term>salitter</term> <note>A term used by the mystic Jacob Boehme to describe the divine "sap" or the living, spiritual substance of the universe.</note> in the heaven of nature was the first in its ascent, within the light of love. For every <term>source-spirit</term> <note>original: "source-esprit." These are the seven active forces or "fountain-spirits" that drive the workings of nature and the divine life.</note> in celestial nature contains within itself the mode and properties of all the other source-spirits, since each is always permeated by the others. From this results the life and the inscrutable generation of God. But this source-spirit takes the first rank because of its power, and that is its own *body*, from which it derives its name.

88. Thus, every <term>source-spirit</term> possesses the property of the whole of nature; and at the time of the ignition of wrath <note>In this cosmology, the "ignition of wrath" refers to the moment spiritual forces became harsh and contracted, forming the material world.</note>, their operations were thus incorporated into death; and from these various operations of spirit came the earth, stones, minerals, and water.

89. That is why you find minerals, stones, water, and earth according to the quality of each spirit within the ground; and it is for this reason that the earth has so many different qualities, all according to which <term>source-spirit</term> was predominant, through its actual generation, at the moment of the ignition.

90. Nature has likewise revealed this multiplicity to man, so that he may know how to dissolve and remove foreign matter from the heterogeneous and corrupt generation of each <term>source-spirit</term>, by which means that same source-spirit might regain its predominance in its own <term>primacy</term> <note>original: "primatie." This refers to the original, pure, and leading state of a spirit.</note>.

91. You have an example of this in gold and silver. You cannot make them pure at the...

<summary>This page describes how the spiritual "source-spirits" were frozen into material forms like minerals and earth during a cosmic fall, creating the diverse qualities of the physical world. It suggests that by understanding these spiritual origins, humans can learn to purify matter—specifically gold and silver—back to its original state.</summary>

# CHAPTER TWENTY-THIRD.

## On the depth, or of the space above the earth.

1. When man considers the depth above the earth, he sees nothing but the stars <note>original: *astres*; refers generally to celestial bodies including planets and suns.</note> and clouds of water. Then he thinks there must be another place where the divinity manifests in a celestial and angelic order. He would simply like to distinguish the divinity from space and the laws that govern it; for he sees nothing there but the stars; and what lies in between is fire, air, and water.

2. Then he thinks it entered into the plans of the divinity to make things thus out of nothing. <note>This refers to the theological concept of *creatio ex nihilo*, the belief that God created the universe from nothingness.</note> But, he says to himself, how can God be in this substance? Or else, how could this substance be God himself? He always imagines that there is a dwelling place in which God reigns and dwells with his spirits; that God cannot be a God whose being consists in the **virtue** <note>In 17th-century philosophical contexts, "virtue" (*vertu*) refers to an inherent power, efficacy, or active force rather than just moral character.</note> of this **visible** government. <note>By "visible government," the author refers to the observable natural world and the laws of physics/astronomy.</note>

3. Several have dared to say: What kind of God would it be whose **body**, being, and power resided in fire, air, water, and earth?

4. See, you intelligent man, I wish to show you the true basis of the divinity. If the universality of things is not God, then you<unclear>s</unclear>

<vocab>divinity, celestial order, angelic, stars, fire, air, water, earth, visible government, universality</vocab>

<summary>The author examines the human tendency to separate God from the physical elements of the universe, arguing against the idea that the divine dwells only in a distant, separate realm.</summary>
<keywords>celestial depth, four elements, divine substance, visible government, omnipresence, cosmology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument from Chapter 23 regarding the omnipresence of God within the material and spatial world. The previous page questioned how God could be within the physical elements; this page responds by asserting that if God were separate from substance, humanity would have no connection to the divine at all.</meta>

<header>CH. 23.</header>
<page-num>237</page-num>

[you] are not the image of God. If there were another, foreign God, then you would have no part in him. For you are created from this God, you live in him, and it is this same God of substance from whom you continually receive strength, blessings, food, and drink; all your **knowledge** <note>original: *connoissance*; in this context, it refers to both intellectual knowledge and conscious awareness.</note> also resides in this same God; and when you die, it is in this same God that you are buried.

5. Now, if there were a foreign God who existed outside of this one, who then would bring you back to life again outside of this God in whom you dissolve? How would a foreign God, by whom you were not created and in whom you never lived, configure and reunite your body and your spirit?

6. If you were of a different substance than God himself, how could you be his child? Or how could Christ—man and king—be the physical son of God, whom he generated from his heart?

7. Now, if his divinity were a different being from his **circumscription** <term>circumscription: The state of being limited by a boundary or form. The author is arguing against the idea that God’s "body" or visible manifestation is separate from his "heart" or inner essence.</term>, then there would be two divinities in him; his circumscription or his body would belong to the God of this world, and his heart would belong to the unknown God.

8. O you, son of man! Open the eyes of your spirit; I wish to show you here the true, exact gate—the very gate of divinity—just as this same unique God wants it to be.

9. Look. Such is the true, unique God from whom you were created, and in whom you live. When you consider space, the stars, and the earth,

<summary>The author argues for the essential unity of God and the physical world, claiming that if God were a "foreign" entity separate from our substance, resurrection and divine parentage would be impossible. He urges the reader to see the divine within the structure of the universe rather than as something distant or external.</summary>

...then you see your God; you live, and you are in this same God, and this same God governs you as well, and it is from this same God that you likewise receive your thoughts; you are a creature of him and in him, otherwise you would be nothing.

10. Now, will you say that I write as a pagan? Listen, look, and notice the difference, and how all things are; for I do not write as a pagan, but as a philosopher. Nor am I a pagan, for I possess the depth and the true **knowledge** <note>original: *connoissance*; here implying a deep, experiential spiritual awareness rather than just intellectual facts.</note> of the unique great God who is everything.

11. When you consider space, the stars, the elements, the earth, you do not grasp pure and clear divinity, and although it is indeed there in all these things, nonetheless you see and you grasp first with your eyes death, then wrath, and the **infernal fire** <note>In the mystical tradition this text represents, "infernal fire" or wrath refers to the harsh, consuming quality of nature when separated from divine love.</note>.

12. But if you lift up your thoughts, and you consider where God is, then you grasp the **sidereal generation** <term>sidereal generation: The process by which the qualities of the stars (sidereal) influence the "birth" or manifestation of life and spirit in the physical world.</term> where love and wrath are in action one against the other. But if you draw faith from the God who governs in holiness, in this empire; then you penetrate through the heaven, and you reach God in his holy heart.

13. Now, when that happens, you are like the **universal God** who is himself the heaven, the earth, the stars and the elements, and you have a similar order of things within you, and you are also a person similar to what the universal God is in the place of this world.

<summary>The author describes the mystical unity between the individual and God, asserting that while nature contains "wrath" and "fire," faith allows the soul to reach God's holy heart. ->Now you will say:<-

> How can I understand this, since the kingdom of God, that of hell, and that of the demon are separated from one another, and cannot be a single body? Likewise, if the earth and the stones are not God, nor the sky and the stars, nor the elements, how can man be God even less so? Otherwise, could he not have been rejected by God?

14. Here I will explain the foundations to you one after the other; keep the question in your mind.

### *Of the sidereal generation and the divine generation.* <note>The term "generation" (original: *engendrement*) here refers to the process of birth or "bringing forth" existence from the divine essence.</note>

15. Before the time of the created heaven, and of the stars, and of the elements, and before the creation of the angels, there was no wrath of God, nor any death, nor any demon, nor earth, nor stone; nor was there any star. But the divinity engendered itself sweetly and graciously, and configured itself into images that were **corporized** <note>Meaning they were given a "body" or substantial form, though not yet a material one.</note> according to the **source-spirits** <term>source-spirits: These are the seven fundamental active qualities or "wellsprings" (German: *Quellgeister*) that make up the divine nature and all subsequent creation.</term>, through their generation, their struggle, and their ascension. They also dissipated again through this same struggle, and configured themselves into another form, all according to the form in which each source-spirit was predominant, as you have read above.

16. But pay close attention here. The severe and strong generation from which came the wrath of God, hell, and death, was indeed from all eternity in

<summary>This page addresses the paradox of how a holy God, a dark hell, and a material world can coexist, beginning an explanation of the "generation" of the universe. The author describes a pre-creation state where the divinity existed in a harmonious, shifting play of "source-spirits" before the emergence of wrath or physical matter.</summary>

...God, but not as an ignition or an ascension. For the universal God consists of seven species, or sevenfold forms or generations. If these generations did not exist, there would be no God, nor any life, nor any angel, nor any individual creature.

17. These same *generations* <note>original: "engendremens"</note> have no beginning, but they have birthed themselves in this way from all eternity. In this profound depth, God himself does not know what he is. For he knows no beginning, nor anything similar to himself, nor any end.

18. These seven generations are in everything; none is the first, nor any the second, the third, or the last; but all seven are each simultaneously the first, the second, the third, and the last. However, I must place them one after the other according to our creaturely <note>The term "creaturely" (original: "créaturel") refers to the limited perspective and language of created beings, which requires linear logic even when describing eternal, non-linear truths.</note> mode and language, otherwise you would not understand me; for the divinity is like a wheel formed of seven wheels one inside the other, where one sees neither beginning nor end.

->*Now observe.*<-

19. First, there is the **astringent quality** <term>astringent quality: The first of the seven source-spirits, representing the force of contraction, cold, and "drawing together" that allows spirit to take on form or substance.</term> which is continually engendered from the other six spirits; it is in itself hard, cold, and stinging, similar to salt, and even more stinging; for its harsh property cannot be sufficiently grasped by a creature, since in a creature it does not exist uniquely and alone; but I know its nature, according to the mode of the infernal-inflamed quality—

<summary>The author describes the divine nature as a beginningless, eternal cycle of seven "generations" or spirits, using the metaphor of a wheel within wheels. He begins to define the first quality, the "astringent" force, which provides the initial contraction necessary for existence.</summary>

20. The second generation <note>original: "engendrement"</note> is the **sweet water** which is also generated from all six spirits, for it is the sweetness generated from the other six; it springs up within the astringent quality; it continually re-illuminates this astringent quality; it calms and tempers it, so that it does not show its bite, as it would have the power to do in its own harshness, if the power of the water did not oppose it.

21. The third generation is the **bitterness** <term>bitterness: The third source-spirit, representing a stinging, restless force that causes movement and prevents the first spirit from becoming a stagnant, frozen void.</term> resulting from the fire contained in the water; for it rubs painfully <note>original: "se froisse péniblement"; implies a grinding or frictional movement that generates energy</note> against the astringent and harsh cold, and it gives activity to the cold. From this comes **mobility**.

22. The fourth generation is the **fire** which results from the mobility or the friction within the astringent spirit; *this fire* from then on is highly ignited, and the bitterness is stinging and ravaging. Now, when the fire-spirit rubs itself thus furiously within the astringent cold, there is then an anguished, frightening, shivering, harsh, and oppositional birthing.

->*Notice here the depth.*<-

the powers, and he is the spirit of life; the powers can no longer reach or grasp him, but he sets them ablaze, and through his bubbling <note>original: "bouillonnement"; here Böhme uses the imagery of a boiling spring or fountain to describe the active, surging vitality of the Holy Spirit</note> he produces forms and images; he shapes them according to the manner in which the generation in struggle <note>Böhme views all existence as a dynamic tension or "combat" between different spiritual qualities that eventually find harmony in the light</note> is found in each place.

70. And if you are not willfully blind, you must know that the air is this same spirit; but in the realm of this world, nature is considerably ignited in the fire of wrath; this was brought about by the sovereign Lucifer <note>Böhme posits that Lucifer’s fall corrupted the "inner" nature of this world, turning the divine fire into a harsh "fire of wrath"</note>; and the Holy Spirit, who is the spirit of gentleness, remains hidden there, in his heaven.

71. You do not need to ask where this same heaven is. It is in your heart. Only open it; the key to it is shown to you here.

72. Thus there is one single God, and three distinct persons, one within the other; and none can grasp or retain the other, or fathom the origin of the other; but the Father begets the Son, and the Son is the heart of the Father, his love and his light; and he is the source of joy, and the beginning of all life.

73. *And the Holy Spirit is the spirit of life; and a shaper and a creator of all things, the one who accomplishes the will in God; who has formed and created from the <term>circumscription</term> <note>original: "circonscription"; referring to the divine sphere or the boundary of God's self-manifestation</note> and within the <term>circumscription</term> of the Father, all the angels and all creatures; who still contains and forms everything daily, and who is the clear-sightedness <note>original: "clairvoyance"; in this context, it refers to the Spirit's role as the divine perception or all-seeing wisdom</note> and the living spirit of God; just as the word that the Father expresses from his powers, so it is formed by the Spirit.*

<vocab>Lucifer, Holy Spirit, God, Father, Son, Clear-sightedness, Circumscription</vocab>

<summary>Böhme concludes his description of the Trinity by defining the Holy Spirit as the active agent who gives form to God's will and resides within the human heart. He explains that while the physical world is affected by Lucifer's "fire of wrath," the gentle Holy Spirit remains accessible through inner spiritual awakening.</summary>

### *Of the great simple quality of God.*

74. Come here, *fool*, you who travel from heaven into the hells, and from the hells even into death, in which is found the sting of the demon; look at yourself, you, a man wise according to the world, who leans upon a wicked wisdom.

75. Pay attention to this, you, learned jurists <note>original: "savans juristes"; Böhme often critiqued those who applied rigid, literalist legal logic to spiritual matters</note> of the world. If you do not wish to come before this mirror, before the clear and pure face of God, and contemplate yourselves there, then the spirit will present to you the *begetting* <note>original: "l’engendrement"; refers to the eternal process by which God and nature are manifested</note> in the innermost astringent circle <term>astringent circle: the first of Böhme's seven "qualities" of nature, representing the contractive, harsh, and cold force that gives structure but can become wrathful</term> where discernment is begotten, where there is the harshness of the anguished *begetting* of God; for it is in that very place that your penetrating and profound intelligence is begotten.

76. If you wish then to be Gods and not demons, make use of the holy and gentle laws of God; otherwise, you will beget yourselves unceasingly and eternally in the harsh and severe begetting of God; it is the spirit and not my mortal flesh that says this as a word from God.

77. You must know that I do not draw at all from dead reason, but that my spirit *inqualifies with God* <term>inqualify: from the German "mitqualifizieren," meaning to act or vibrate in unison with the same qualities or divine energies</term>, and experiences the divinity, and tastes and feels how it is in all its *begettings*. And by this I find that the divinity is an entirely simple, gentle, lovable, and fixed being; that the generation of the trinity of God operates quite gently, joyously, deliciously, and in a unanimous concurrence; and that the harsh

<summary>Böhme warns those who rely on worldly intellect that they risk being trapped in the "harsh" aspect of God's nature, contrasting this with his own direct experience of God as a gentle, simple, and harmonious Trinity.</summary>

78. Now, this hidden harshness is called the wrath of God; and the essence of sweetness within the trinity is called God. There, nothing comes forth from the harshness that corrupts or ignites anger; instead, the spirits play together most amicably, like little children who rejoice among themselves; who each have their own activity there; who recreate together, and caress one another.

79. It is so among the holy angels; and there is in the trinity of God a sweet, gracious, and delicious being, in which the spirit always rises in tone; and one power touches the other; it is as if there were harmonious hymns and melodious concerts there.

80. And just as this ascension is in every point or every place, so too the tone is formed there; but entirely softly, in a way that is imperceptible to the *bodies* of the angels, but which their *soul-based* <note>original: "animique"; referring to the "soul-quality" or the inner spiritual essence rather than the external form</note> begetting can grasp; and just as the divinity manifests at every point or every place, so too the angel manifests there. For the angels are created by this essence, and they have among them their princes of the source-spirits <term>source-spirits: Böhme's "Quellgeister," the seven primary forces of nature (such as sweetness, bitterness, and heat) that flow into one another to create all existence</term> just as they are in the begetting of God.

...ingenious discoveries that bring honor, credit, and wealth.

->*Reply.*<-

84. If you follow me along this line through which I have pierced into the depths of God, then you have taken firm steps. I did not enter into this doctrine, into this labor, or into this knowledge through my own reason, nor by a project of my own will. I did not seek these sciences at all, and I was entirely ignorant of them. I sought only the heart of God, to shelter myself within Him from the storms of the demon <note>Böhme frequently describes his writing as a "spiritual labor" or a struggle against the "tempests" of doubt and the devil</note>.

85. But when I was introduced into this knowledge, then this great and laborious task was imposed upon me: to manifest and reveal to the world the great day of the Lord; to discover to men what the whole tree is <note>Böhme uses the "Tree of Life" or the "Whole Tree" as a metaphor for the totality of existence, including both the divine and the natural worlds</note>, since they so ardently seek the root of the tree, and by this means, to announce to them that it is the dawn of the day which God has long ago decreed in His counsel. Amen.

86. Thus you see now what divinity is, how its love and its wrath have been from all eternity, and how its generation <term>generation: in Böhme’s theology, this refers to the eternal process of "birth" by which God's qualities manifest from the dark abyss into light</term> occurs. You must not say that you are not within divinity or that you do not live within it, nor that divinity is something foreign to which you cannot attain; for where you are, there is the door of God. Therefore, if you are holy, you are then near to God in heaven as far as your soul is concerned. If you are wicked, you are then, as for your soul, in the infernal fire.

->*Now extend your observation.*<-

87. When God created the angels all at once, they were produced from this divine generation <term>generation: the eternal process by which God's essence takes form and comes to life</term>: their *body* or their circumscription <note>circumscription: the boundary or limited form that defines an individual being</note> was a contraction of nature, in which their spirit and their light were engendered, just as the divinity was engendered. And just as the source-spirits of God continually draw their strength and their power from the *body* of nature; so also do the angels constantly receive their strength and their power from the nature of God.

88. And just as the holy spirit forms everything and configures everything in nature; so also the spirit of the angels *inqualifies* <term>inqualifies: from the German 'inqualicieren,' meaning to operate within or permeate the qualities and forces of another being</term> within the holy spirit, and helps to form and configure everything, so that everything is but one heart and one will, pure delights, and *true joy*.

89. For the angels are the children of the great God, whom he has engendered in his *body* of nature, for the increase of divine joy.

90. But here you must know that the *bodies* of the angels cannot reach the divine generation; thus their *body* does not understand it. Only their *spirit* understands it, but the *body* stops *there*, just as nature does in God, and it allows the spirit to work and play lovingly with God. For the angels play before God and with God, like little children before their parents, by which means the divine joy increases.

## CHAPTER TWENTY-FOUR.

->Of the compaction of the stars.<-

1. WHEN the entire body of nature within the space of this world was numb, as if in a powerful death, and yet life was hidden within it; then God set the entire body of nature of this world into motion on the fourth day. From that nature, and from the light that was ascending, he engendered the stars; for the wheel of divine generation <term>divine generation: the eternal process by which God’s qualities or 'source-spirits' interact to bring forth life and light</term> moved once again, just as it had done from all eternity.

2. It had indeed moved, it is true, on the first day, and it had begun the engendering within the body of corrupted nature; this is why, on the first day, life separated itself from death. On the second day, a firmament <note>original: "firmament," referring here to the division between the waters or the psychological/spiritual boundary between realms</note> was created between them; and on the third day, life pierced through death. For then the light broke through the darkness, and rendered the dead body of nature blooming and mobile.

3. Indeed, on the third day the body of nature suffered great anguish <note>original: "s'engoissa," describing the 'Angst' or labor pains of nature as it struggles to bring forth light from darkness</note>, to the point that the fire of love was kindled within death, and the light of life pierced through the frozen body of death, and bloomed from the womb of death. But on the third day it remained only

## CHAPTER TWENTY-FOUR.

### On the Compaction <term>compaction: refers here to the condensing or solidification of spiritual forces into physical celestial bodies</term> of the Stars.

1. When the entire body of nature within the space of this world was numbed as if in a mighty death, and yet life was hidden within it; then God set in motion the entire body of the nature of this world, on the fourth day, and from nature—from the light that was ascending—he generated the stars; for the wheel of divine generation <term>divine generation: the perpetual, cyclic process by which God’s internal qualities manifest as outward life and form</term> moved once again, as it had done from all eternity.

2. It had indeed moved, it is true, on the first day, and it had begun the engendering within the body of corrupted nature; therefore, on the first day, life separated from death; on the second day, a firmament was created between them; and on the third day, life pierced through death. For then the light pierced through the darkness, and rendered the dead body of nature flowering and mobile.

3. Indeed, on the third day the body of nature experienced a great anguish <note>original: "s'engoissa," a key concept in this philosophy where "anguish" (Angst) represents the intense contraction or birth-pang necessary for life to break out of darkness</note>, to the point that the fire of love was kindled in death, and the light of life pierced through the frozen body of death, and flowered from the womb of death. But on the third day it remained only

It sets the stage for the creation of the stars on the fourth day as a fulfillment of this process.</summary>

<keywords>creation, fourth day, stars, divine generation, firmament, light and darkness, nature, birth-pang</keywords>

4. Now, on the fourth day, the light made its ascension and established its seat in the house of death; however, death could not reach it. Just as the harsh generation of God <note>This refers to the "Qualities" of God—specifically the first three properties of nature (contraction, friction, and fire) which feel "harsh" or "bitter" before they are transformed by the light.</note> which resides in the innermost core, and from which life results, cannot reach the sweetness, nor the light of sweetness, nor the spirit which is in sweetness; likewise, the dead darkness of this world, nor any demon, can reach the light of nature.

5. But the light shines through death, and it has established its royal seat in the midst, in the house of death and of the wrath of God; and it generates for itself, out of the house of wrath, a new body of God, which subsists eternally in the love of God, but is elusive <note>original: "insaisissable," meaning it cannot be grasped or perceived by the lower senses</note> to the old body inflamed in the outermost generation.

->Now you will ask:<-

->How can I understand this?<-

6. earthly comparison, so that you may enter a little into the deep meaning.

7. Consider a tree whose outer bark is hard and coarse, as if dead and extinguished, and yet it is not entirely in death, but merely without vigor. Now, there is a difference between this bark and the body <note>By "body," the author refers to the inner wood and sap-carrying heart of the tree.</note> that grows beneath the bark. For the body has its living strength and pierces through the dried bark, and generates for itself many beautiful young buds, which nevertheless all remain attached to the old body.

8. But the bark is like a dead thing, and cannot reach the life of the tree; it is merely suspended from it; it is a covering for the tree in which worms nest, and by this means they also eventually destroy the tree.

9. It is the same with the entire house of this Universe; the outermost darknesses are the house of the wrath of God in which the demons dwell; and it is truly the house of death, for the holy light of God is dead there. (Understand that it is compressed in its principle, and the outermost <term>substantiality: the "matter" or spiritual substance that forms the body of a being or world</term> in God must be regarded as dead; and yet in God it is living, but in another source.)

27. 28. But the fact that there are so many stars, with such manifold operations, comes from infinity, which—through a concourse of reciprocal actions—imprints itself into the seven spirits of God, and which engenders itself without end. <note>The "seven spirits of God" (or *Quellgeister* in the original German) are Böhme's core concept of the seven fundamental forces or qualities of nature that drive all existence, from the dark and harsh to the light and gentle.</note>

29. As for the fact that the generation, or the body of the stars, does not change its position, according to what it has done from all eternity, this signifies that there must be a permanent engendering. By this means, the numb body of the earth must be ignited anew in a uniform yet existing operation within infinity, and be engendered once more, as well as the house of deep darkness above the earth. Thus, the new body could be ceaselessly engendered from death, until time was fulfilled and the entire body had taken birth.

->Now you will say<-

> *If the stars are indeed God, must they then be prayed to and honored as God?*

30. It is also this far that the pagan sages reached, who indeed, through their subtle intelligence, far surpassed our philosophers; but the true door of knowledge remained closed to them. <note>Böhme likely refers here to ancient Greek, Egyptian, or Hermetic thinkers who recognized the divinity of the cosmos but, in his view, mistook the "body" of the world for the Creator Himself.</note>

31. Look. The stars are indeed conglomerated by God, but you must understand the difference between them and Him. For they are not the heart, nor the pure and gentle divinity that one must honor and adore as God; but they are the generation

32. But the harsh generation of the stars cannot reach back to the heart of God, nor to the holy spirit. However, the light of God—which rises in anguish <note>Böhme uses "anguish" (French: *angoisse*, German: *Angst*) to describe the intense, driving tension or "contraction" of the fundamental forces of nature before they break through into the light of divine joy.</note> through the combined bubbling of the holy spirit—remains free before itself, like the heart, and reigns in the middle, within the enclosure of the hidden heaven, which comes from the water of life.

33. For it is from this same heaven that the stars received their ignition, and they are but a simple instrument that God employs for the *engendering.*

34. This is a central term for Böhme, meaning that two things share the same qualities or act together in a single divine nature.</note> with the pure divinity, as a member with its body.

35. Thus the heart or the light of God is also ceaselessly engendered in the *body* of this world, and this same engendered heart makes but one heart with the beginningless and eternal heart of God, who, there, is in and above all heavens.

36. It is not only engendered in and from the stars, but in the entire *body* of this world. Now, the stars continually ignite the *body* of this world, so that generation takes place everywhere.

->*But here you must observe this well.*<-

37. The light, or the heart of God, does not take its origin solely and plainly from the harsh and coarse stars, where, indeed, love and wrath are within one another, but rather from the seat where the sweet water of life is unceasingly generated. <note>In theosophical tradition, the "sweet water of life" represents the pure, divine substance that exists prior to the harshness of material nature.</note>

38. For this same water is not reached by death in the kindling of wrath, but it endures for all eternity; it extends to every point of this world, and it is the water of life that pierces through death. It is from this that the new body of God is rebuilt in this world.

39. It is in the stars just as much as in all other points; nevertheless, it is nowhere graspable or perceptible. Yet it fills everything equally at once. It is also in the body of man, and when he thirsts for this water and drinks of it, then the light of life, which is the heart of God, is kindled in him, and at that instant the Holy Spirit manifests itself there.

->How then can the stars exist in both love and wrath?<-

The stars came forth from the burning house of God’s <term>wrath</term> <note>The term "wrath" (French: *colère*) here refers to the dark, fiery, and contractive principle of nature that precedes the light of divine love.</note>, which is symbolized by the movement of a child in the mother’s womb during the third month. 41. But when God moved within the body of this world, then, on the third day, the <term>anguish</term> <note>The French "angoisse" (corresponding to Böhme's German *Qual*) refers to a state of intense, churning labor or tension within nature as it struggles to bring forth life from the dark, fiery principle.</note> was stirred in the *birth* of this world; from this resulted the fire-flash, and the light of the stars was ignited in the water of life. 42. 43. Thus, the newborn love which has risen from the water of life into the stars and into the whole body of this world is bound with eternal love and

44. No. But it remains in its birth most harsh, most severe, and most agonizing, in which the wrath of God extends without interruption, as in hellfire.

45. For if nature, with its <term>harsh</term> <note>original: "âpre." In Böhme’s system, the "harsh" or "astringent" quality (German: *herb*) is the first property of nature—the force of contraction and darkness.</note> birth, had indeed and entirely changed into love, according to the celestial law, the demons would once again be in the holy seat of God.

46. You can also see and understand this very well by the terrible power of heat and cold, as well as by the poison of bitterness and harshness in this world; all things which are in the birth of the stars, in which the demons remain captive.

47. The stars are only the igniting of the great house, for the universal house is numbed in death just like the earth; given that the outermost birth is dead and numbed, just as the bark is on a tree; but the <term>sidereal birth</term> <note>The "sidereal" or "astral" birth refers to the living forces of the stars that animate the material body of the world.</note> is the body in which the inner life rises.

48. Now, within its body, it is entirely harsh. However, the <term>new birth</term> <note>original: "nouvel engendrement." This is the spiritual regeneration through the "water of life" that allows the soul or nature to transcend its dark origins.</note> which rises in the water of life and which pierces through death

### CH. 24. ( 273 )
II. 18

makes it sweet. However, it cannot change the nucleus of the <term>harsh generation</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "harsh generation" refers to the "astringent" or "bitter" qualities of nature that provide the structure for existence but can become "wrathful" if not balanced by light.</note>, but it is generated apart from it. It keeps within itself its new holy life; it pierces through the wrathful death, and the wrathful death does not reach it at all.

49. Now this love and this wrath make but one body, but the <term>water of life</term> <note>A spiritual element that acts as a buffer or medium between the dark fire of God's wrath and the light of His love.</note> is the heaven of separation between them, so that in this way love does not seize or contain the wrath within itself, nor likewise does the wrath contain the love; but love rises in the water of life, and it receives from the first and harsh *generation* <note>original: "engendrement"</note> the power which is in the light, and which is generated from the wrath, so that the new *body* is generated from the old. For the old *body* which is in the harsh generation belongs to the demon as his dwelling place, and the new belongs to the kingdom of Christ.

->*Now the question is asked:*<-

->*Are the three persons of the divinity not then presently in the generation of sweetness in this world?*<-

50. Yes, all three are in this world, in the full generation of love, sweetness, holiness, and purity, and are unceasingly generated in the same substance and the same being where they have been for all eternity.

51. See. God the Father said to the people of Israel on Mount Sinai, when He gave them the law:

> I am a severe and jealous God, for those who hate me (Exodus 20:5, Deuteronomy 5:9).

<summary>This page explains how divine love and wrath coexist in the world as a single "body" yet remain separated by the "water of life," allowing for a new spiritual life to be born out of the old, harsh nature. It affirms that the Holy Trinity exists fully within the world's "sweetness" despite the ongoing presence of divine wrath.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the divine Trinity as an active, generative process within nature, further distinguishing between the "harsh" structural forces of the Father and the "sweet" light of the Son.</meta>

<header>( 274 ) CH. 24.</header>

52. Now, from this single father who is here both severe and full of love, you must not make two persons; rather, it is one and only one father who continually begets his beloved son; and from these two, the holy spirit unceasingly proceeds.

->*Note the depth in the center.*<-

53. The father is the unique being who is himself everything, and who, from all eternity, begets his beloved son, and in the two of them the holy spirit has always been in explosion <note>The French *explosion* here likely translates the German *Ausbruch*, referring to the "breaking forth" or "outflowing" of the Spirit.</note>, there where life is generated.

54. But from the harsh and rough generation of the <term>source-spirits</term> <note>Translating *sources-esprits* (German: *Quellgeister*). These are the seven foundational qualities or active forces—such as "astringency" or "bitterness"—that Böhme believes constitute the inner life of both God and nature.</note> of the father, in which heat and severity reside, the body of nature unceasingly proceeds; in this body exists the light of the son, or the heart of the father, which is ungraspable to nature itself.

55. For, in the generation, the light is in the middle, and is the seat of life. It is there that the sweet life of God is generated from all the powers of the father; and in this same place or at this same point, the holy spirit rises from the father and the son.

56. Now, these same powers of the father, which exist in the <term>kindling</term> <note>The original French *enflammement* refers to the moment the divine light ignites the dark, fiery potential of the Father's "harsh" qualities, turning them into holy light.</note> of the light, are the holy father, and the sweet father, and the pure divine generation; and the spirit that rises there is the holy spirit; but the harsh generation is the body, in which this holy life is perpetually generated.

<summary>This page explains the Trinity as a dynamic process where the "harsh" structural forces of the Father (the body of nature) provide the foundation for the "sweet" generation of the Son (the Light) and the Holy Spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Source-Spirits, Divine Light, Generation of God, Heart of the Father, Jacob Böhme, Mysticism, Nature of God</keywords>

<header>CH. 24. ( 275 )</header>

57. However, when the light of God shines through this harsh generation <note>French: *engendrement âpre*. In Jacob Böhme’s thought, this refers to the raw, contractionary, and "stern" forces of the Father that precede the "light" of the Son.</note>, it then becomes sweet, and it is like a sleeping man in whom life moves unceasingly while the body is in a sweet rest.

58. Now, the kindling <note>French: *enflammement*; German: *Anzündung*. This refers to the moment the divine light ignites the potential energy of the dark "source-spirits."</note> was also in this body of nature; for from this same body the angels were also created, and if they had not exalted and inflamed themselves in their audacity, their bodies would have remained eternally in a peaceful and ungraspable sweetness, as in the other principalities of the angels outside of this world; their spirit would have unceasingly generated itself in their body of sweetness, just as the holy trinity does in the body of God, and this innate or *in-generated* <note>The French *in-engendré* suggests a spirit that exists as an inherent part of the divine flow rather than something created as a separate, external object.</note> spirit would have been but one heart, one will, and one love with the holy trinity; for they had also been created in the midst, in the body or the circumscription of God, for the joy of the divinity.

59. But the sovereign Lucifer wanted to be himself the all-powerful God, and he inflamed his body, or his sphere of activity; he awakened within it the harsh generation of God, and he set himself in opposition to the luminous heart of God, with the intention of reigning over it through his imperious will, which however was impossible.

60. But as he inflamed himself and rose up against the law of the divinity, then the harsh generation also rose up against him in the body of the father, and took him prisoner in the harsh generation, like a furious son, and that is where his eternal sovereignty now lies.

<header>CH. 24.</header>

61. But when the Father became inflamed in the body of harshness <note>French: *âpreté*. In Böhme’s system, this refers to the "stern" or "astringent" quality of the first divine properties, often associated with the Father's power before it is softened by the Son's light.</note>, he did not for that reason inflame the holy source where his heart full of love is generated; it was not as if, by this means, his heart had to be placed within the source of wrath.

62. No. That was impossible, for the harsh generation cannot reach the holy and pure generation. Instead, the holy generation pierces through the other, and generates for itself a new body which subsists once more in sweetness.

63. And this same new body is the water of life, which is generated when the light traverses the wrath; and it is the Holy Spirit who is the shaper <note>French: *formateur*. The Holy Spirit acts as the active, organizing principle that gives shape to the divine essence.</note> within it. But heaven is the separation between love and wrath, and it is the seat where wrath is transformed into love.

64. Now, when you look at the sun and the stars, you must not believe that they are the holy and pure God, and you must not intend to offer them anything, nor to ask them for anything; for they are not the holy God. Rather, they are the harsh generation, inflamed from his body, in which love and wrath struggle against one another.

65. But the holy God is hidden in his heaven, in the midst of all these things, and they can neither reach him nor understand him. However, the soul seizes him; and the sideric generation <note>French: *génération sidérique*. From the Latin *sidus* (star). This refers to the "astral" or "starry" spirit that Böhme believed governed the physical world and the human senses, distinct from the eternal soul.</note> only half-comprehends him, for heaven is the separation between love and wrath. This same heaven is everywhere, and it is also within yourself.

66. When you pray to the holy God in his heaven, you pray to him in the heaven that is within you; this same

Böhme warns against worshipping the stars (the physical universe), noting that while they are part of God's "body," the true, holy God remains hidden and accessible only through the soul within oneself.</summary>

# CHAPTER TWENTY-FIFTH.

->**Of the entire body of the birth of the stars; that is, universal astrology, or the entire body of this world.**<-

1. The scholars and masters experienced in astrology <note>In the 17th-century context of Jakob Böhme, "astrology" and "astronomy" were often used interchangeably to describe the study of the heavens and their influence on Earth.</note> have penetrated so far and so deeply into their art that they know the course and the operations of the stars; what their conjunction signifies; what their influence and the expansion of their powers can produce; how this brings the winds, rain, snow, and heat, as well as good and evil; good and bad fortune, as well as life and death, and finally everything that stimulates and agitates this world.

2. Indeed, this is the true foundation that I recognize in the spirit to be such. But their science resides only in the house of death <note>The "house of death" refers to the material, perishable world that can be perceived by the senses, as opposed to the eternal, spiritual reality.</note>; only in the most outward [*tangibility*] <note>original: "saisissabilité." This likely translates the German term *Greiflichkeit*, referring to the "graspable" or material nature of the world.</note>; only in observation through the eyes of the body; and the root of this tree has remained hidden from them until now.

3. Therefore, I do not intend to treat the branches of the tree, nor to overturn their knowledge; nor do I build upon their ground; but I let their science rest upon its seat, especially since I have not studied it at all.

<summary>In this opening to Chapter 25, the author acknowledges the validity of traditional astrology while arguing that it only understands the external, material effects of the stars. He intends to reveal the "root" or spiritual origin of the cosmos, which he claims remains hidden from worldly scholars.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, stars, house of death, tangibility, cosmology, spiritual root, celestial influence</keywords>

CH. 25. ( 281 )

And it is in the spirit of my knowledge <note>original: "connoissances." In Böhme’s terminology, this refers to experiential spiritual insight rather than academic learning.</note> that I write about the root of the tree, about its trunk, its branches, and its fruit (as a laborer works for his master), in order to reveal the entire tree of this world.

4. My intention is not to give currency to something new, for I have received no command for that; but my knowledge pierces into this birth <note>original: "engendrement." This refers to the generative process by which the cosmos is constantly being "born" from the divine source.</note> of the stars, into the medium where life is generated and passes through death, and where the boiling spirit exists and makes a breach; and it is indeed by its impulse and through its boiling that I write.

5. I also know well that the children of the flesh will mock me, and say that I should have waited for my vocation; that I should not torment myself so much over these things; that I should think rather, and with more care, of providing for my subsistence and that of my family, and leave philosophy to those who have studied it, and who are called to it.

6. It is through these assaults as well that the devil has frequently attacked me; he so dazzled me with such insinuations that I often formed the resolution to abandon everything; but my first project prevailed. For when I occupied myself with my animal life <note>Böhme uses "animal life" (vie animale) to describe the physical, instinctual, and material existence of man, as opposed to his spiritual life.</note>, and when I internally resolved to leave my undertaking behind, the gates of heaven closed to me in my knowledge.

7. Then my soul was in anguish, as if it had been imprisoned by the devil. This is why my thought has experienced so many shocks,

<summary>Böhme explains that his writing is driven by a "boiling spirit" and describes his struggle against the temptation to abandon his spiritual work for a conventional material life.</summary>

### CH. 25

...to the point that my body was ready to perish from it; and my spirit would take no rest until it had pierced once again through dead reason <note>original: "raison morte." Böhme uses this term to describe human logic that is disconnected from divine revelation and spiritual life.</note>; until it had broken the gates of darkness and restored itself to its seat and its place: thereby it has always received a new life and a new strength.

8. By this means, I understood that the spirit must be tested by the cross and affliction; thus I have not lacked for bodily trials, and I have had to be continually in combat, so much has the devil been set against my work.

9. But as I perceived that my eternal salvation <term>salut éternel: The state of being saved from sin and death to live eternally with God.</term> depended on it, and that through my discouragement I was closing the gates of light—which is nevertheless the firmament <term>firmament: In Böhme's mysticism, this refers to a stable spiritual boundary or expanse that protects the soul.</term> of my heaven, where my soul takes shelter from the devil’s storms; and as I have nonetheless conquered this light through great exhaustion and in several violent storms, by the love of God and the triumph of my savior and king Jesus Christ—I then leave everything to God’s governance, and I hand over the reason of my flesh <note>original: "raison de ma chair." This refers to the carnal, worldly intellect that resists spiritual truth.</note> to Him as a prisoner.

10. And I have decided for the gates of the knowledge of light; I wish to follow the impulse and the knowledge of the spirit; and even if my fleshly body should be reduced to extreme poverty, or even perish, I do not concern myself with that, and I wish to say with the prophet David:

> Even though my body and my soul should fail, yet God is my salvation, my trust, and the assurance of my heart (Ps. 73:26).

CH. 25.

Luke 19.)? I would have received now what is mine with the profits. And then he would take the talent from me and give it to another who would have better put his own to use; and so I want to sow; it is for him to water, I deliver the whole to his care.

### CH. 25.

18. Regarding the appearance of a blue circle above the stars, which seems to separate the location of this world from the holy heaven—as men have believed until now—it is not so; rather, it is the upper water of nature <note>A reference to the "waters above the firmament" mentioned in Genesis 1:7</note>, which is much purer than the water below the moon; and when the sun shines through space, it appears as a blue color.

19. No one knows how vast and deep the expanse of this world is. And although some physicists or astrologers <note>In the 17th century, "physicists" were natural philosophers studying the physical world, while "astrologers" often included those we would now call astronomers.</note> have undertaken to measure space with their circles or instruments, nonetheless their measures are only conjectural, or rather measurements of groping in the dark, as if someone wanted to grasp the wind.

20. But the dwelling of the demon is now from the moon down to and within the earth, in deep caverns and in cavities, particularly in wild and desert places, and where the earth is very stony and worked by the **bitter quality** <term>bitter quality: a fundamental cosmic force representing contraction, harshness, and the "sting" of life, which can turn into wrath if not balanced by light</term>.

### CH. <note>Abbreviation for Chapter (Chapitre).</note>

...only to show you how the body of the world originated, how it is still at present, and what its governance is.

22. The entire body of this world is like the human body. For it is surrounded in its outermost circle by the stars and by the powers of nature that have developed; and in this body, it is the seven spirits of nature that govern, and the heart of nature is in the middle <note>In this mystical cosmology, the "heart of nature" usually refers to the Sun, which provides life and light to the physical world just as the heart does for the body.</note>.

23. But the stars, in general, are the marvelous proportions and the diversities of God. For when God created the stars, He created them from the rising of infinity, from the ancient body of the divinity, forever ignited.

24. For just as the seven source-spirits <term>source-spirits: The foundational spiritual forces—often categorized as qualities like "bitter," "sweet," or "astringent"—that Böhme believes constitute the divine essence and all created matter.</term> of God generated themselves in infinities, before the time of wrath, through their rising and their inter-qualifying <note>original: "inqualificatiion." This term describes the way the different spiritual qualities "work into" one another or cooperate to create reality.</note> from which originated multitudes of celestial configurations and productions; likewise also the holy God configured His ancient *body* or this corrupted nature into a multitude of powers, such as they had always been in their generation in holiness.

->Conceive well of this profound thing.<-

25. Each star has a particular property, which you can also see in the ornamentation of the earth when it is covered with flowers. And the Creator restored the ancient, ignited body, and made it living within a great multitude of power.

<summary>This page establishes a direct analogy between the human body and the universe, explaining that the stars and the 'seven spirits of nature' govern the world's structure. It describes the physical world as a restoration of the 'ancient body' of divinity, where every star and flower reflects a specific divine property.</summary>

CH. 25.

...so that through this old life, which existed within the wrath, a new life might be generated through the firmament of heaven; so that this new life might possess all the powers and all the <term>potentialities<note>original: "virtualités"</note></term> that it had always held before the time of the wrath; so that it might <term>qualify within<note>The original term "inqualifier" is a mystical concept describing how two spirits or natures intermingle and act as one.</note></term> the pure divinity outside of this world, and that it might be one single holy God with that divinity outside of this world.

26. Likewise, this new birth was flourishing at the time of creation, when man had not yet corrupted it: a crime by which nature was further degraded, and which drew God's curse upon the earth.

27. Once man had seized the fruit of the old body, the fruit of the new body remained hidden in its heaven; man can no longer contemplate it except through his new body, and he cannot enjoy it with his natural body.

28.
