## CH. 2

47. It is the law of divinity that all life is generated in a single way within the body or sphere of activity of God; although this occurs through an immensity of diverse configurations, nevertheless all life has but a single origin.

48. I do not attain such knowledge with my eyes of flesh, but with eyes in which life is generated within me; in this same seat, the gate of heaven and of hell is opened to me. The **new man** <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "new man" is the spiritual self reborn through divine grace, capable of perceiving realities beyond the physical world.</note> meditates in the midst of the <term>sidereal generation: the process by which the stars and celestial bodies influence the birth and qualities of things in the material world</term>; and the inner gate, as well as the outermost, is open to him.

49. But as he is still implanted in the **old man of wrath and death** <note>The "old man" represents the fallen, earthly nature of humanity, subject to physical decay and spiritual "wrath" or distance from God.</note>, and at the same time he sits in his heaven, he therefore sees through both of them; it is in this same way also that he sees the stars and the elements; for in God there is no place that acts as an obstacle, given that the eye of the Lord sees all.

50. If, therefore, my spirit did not see through His spirit, I would be nothing but a blind trunk <note>original: "tronc aveugle." Böhme uses this metaphor to describe a person who is spiritually dead or unresponsive, like a fallen tree log.</note>; but as I see the gates of God open in my spirit, and as I also have the impulse for it, I wish to write exactly according to what is shown to me, and not rely upon the authority of any man.

51. You must not conceive of me as if my **old man** were a living saint or an angel; no, he dwells with all men in the house of wrath and death;

55. But if you want to know the truth about this, I will offer you a comparison. 56. Such is exactly the situation in which you place yourself by your carnal wills. ->Now it will be asked;<-

What is the sun and the other planets? Or rather, how did they come into being?

62. Look. The other planets are specific bodies that have their own physical properties, and are not tied to a fixed place, except for their orbit in which they circulate; but the sun is not a body of this kind; for it is a place ignited by the light of God.

->*Understand this well.*<-

63. The place where the sun is located is a place such as you might choose anywhere on earth. If God wished to ignite the light through heat, the entire world would be exactly like such a sun. 64. For the heat was not as great as it is in the sun, which is why the light was also completely gentle; and it is because of the terrible burning heat <term>ardeur: intense, scorching heat</term> of the sun that it is separated from the gentleness of God. Thus, one should not say that the sun is an open door to the light of God, but that it is like the light in the eye of

### *Of the Planet Mars.*

72. But when the sun was ignited, the terrible explosion of fire shot forth from the place of the sun—out from the sun’s location—like a terrifying and stormy flash of lightning, and carried the fury of the fire into its bodily circumference; it is from this that the water became entirely bitter, and the water is the core or the trunk of the explosion.

73. Now, the astrologers write that the planet Mars is elevated above the sun by 15,750 miles; <note>The author uses contemporary astronomical measurements to ground his spiritual cosmology, though he focuses more on the symbolic significance than the literal distance.</note> I do not contradict this, as I do not concern myself with these measurements. 74. The reason why the light did not seize it sooner was the intensity of the fury and the speed of the flash, for it was not captivated by the light until the light had completely impregnated or subdued it.

75. It is there now like a *tyrant*, a *furious one*, and an *agitator* of the entire *body* of this world, for it has, indeed, the task of agitating everything by its *revolution* in the <term>wheel of nature</term> <note>The "wheel of nature" refers to the cyclical movement of the seven properties of nature that generate all life and manifestation.</note>, from which all life receives its origin.

### *Of the planet Jupiter.*

76. When the bitter flash of fire was imprisoned by the light, then the light by its own power penetrated even further into space, until it reached the hard and cold seat of nature. At that point, the power of the first gushing forth from the sun could not extend further, but it remained there bodily, and it took that same place for its dwelling.

CH. 2

->*But you must understand this exactly.*<-

77. It is the power of the light that remained in this place; it is a being entirely gentle, lovable, and gracious. The astrologers write that *Jupiter* is higher than Mars by 7,875 miles <note>The author continues to use specific, though symbolically charged, distances to describe the spiritual architecture of the heavens.</note>. But it is the moderator of Mars—the destroyer and the furious one; it is the origin of the sweetness of all lives, as well as the origin of the water from which life is engendered, as I shall explain hereafter.

78. It is up to that point that the power of life now extends out from the sun, and no further; but the radiance or splendor, which also has its own power, extends all the way to the stars and penetrates the universal body of this world.

->*But you must specifically understand from where these two planets originated.*<-

79. When the power of the heart of God pierces from the eternal and immortal source of the water of life through the heaven of separation <note>original: "ciel de séparation"; likely referring to the firmament that divides the spiritual waters from the physical realm</note>, and ignited the water in the place of the sun, then the flash (understand this as the flash of fire) leaped out of the water and was extremely violent and terrifying; it is from this that Mars originated.

80. Then the power of the light leaped rapidly after this flash, like a gentle and regenerating life, and it reached the flash of fire and soothed it to such an extent that it became as if without force.

82. It is from this power that the planet **Jupiter** originated—not from the very place where it now exists, but rather it continually ignites that place through its power. Nevertheless, it remains in that place like a servant who must constantly wait upon <note>original: "valeter"; to serve as a valet or attendant, performing duties in a household</note> (by virtue of his office) a house that does not belong to him, whereas the **sun** has a house of its own. Aside from the sun, no other planet possesses its own home.

83. If one wishes, therefore, to firmly establish the generation or the origin of the stars, it is necessary to specifically understand the birthing <note>original: "engendrement"; the spiritual or biological process of being generated or brought into existence</note> of life, and how life is birthed within a body; for in all things, there is only one kind of generation.

84. He who does not know or understand this will likewise not understand the generation of the stars; for the whole of it together is but a single body. When life is birthed within any creature whatsoever, its birthing then resides within its body, just as the birthing of the natural body of this world does; for all life must be birthed according to the law of divinity, and in the manner that divinity perpetually generates itself.

## CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX.

### On the planet Saturn.

1. Saturn, the cold, harsh, austere, and astringent regent, does not take its origin or its extraction from the sun, for it holds within its power the chamber of death, and it is what dries up all the powers; from this results corporality. <note>In Jacob Böhme’s mystical system, Saturn represents the first "quality" of nature—the contractive force that pulls spirit inward to create solid matter.</note>

2. Just as the sun is the heart of life, and an origin of all the spirits in the body of this world; so too is Saturn the one who begins all corporality and all tangibility <note>original: "saisissabilité"; the quality of being able to be grasped or perceived by the physical senses.</note>, and it is in these two stars <note>In the 17th-century context, "stars" (astres) often referred to any celestial body, including planets.</note> that the power of the entire body of this world resides; outside of their power there can be no creature, no configuration, and no mobility within the natural body of this world.

3. But its origin is the severe, astringent, and harsh anguish of the entire body of this world; for when, at the time of the igniting of wrath <note>original: "l’enflammement de la colère"; referring to the Böhmean concept of the Fall, where the divine fire of love turned into a consuming, harsh "wrath" that formed the basis of the material world.</note>, the light was extinguished in the outermost generation of this world (which generation is the transformation into nature, or tangibility <note>original: "saisissabilité"</note>, or the ascension of the birth of all the source-spirits <term>source-spirits: "sources-esprits"; Böhme's term for the seven fundamental forces or "qualities" that generate all of existence</term>) then the astringent quality was, in its birth, most harsh and most austere, and it strongly and violently constricted the operation of all the source-spirits. It is from there

# CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX.

## On the Planet Saturn.

1. Saturn, the cold, harsh, austere, and astringent ruler, does not take its origin or its extraction from the sun; for it holds in its power the chamber of death, and it is he who dries up all powers; from this results **corporeality**. <note>In Böhme’s system, Saturn represents the "First Quality" of nature: contraction. It acts as a cooling, hardening force that turns spiritual energy into physical substance.</note>

2. Just as the sun is the heart of life, and an origin of all the spirits in the body of this world; so too is Saturn the one who begins all corporeality and all <term>tangibility</term> <note>original: *saisissability*; the quality of being able to be grasped or perceived by the senses.</note>, and it is in these two stars that the power of the entire body of this world resides; outside of their power, there can be no creature, no configuration, nor any mobility within the natural body of this world.

3. But its origin is the severe, astringent, and harsh <term>anguish</term> <note>original: *l'angoisse*. In theosophical writing, this refers to a state of "narrowness" or "constriction" (from the Latin *angustus*) where forces are compressed together before they explode into light or matter.</note> of the entire body of this world; for, when at the time of the ignition of wrath, the light was extinguished in the outermost generation of this world (which generation is the transformation into nature, or the **tangibility**, or the ascension of the engendering of all the <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: *sources-esprits*; a translation of Böhme's German *Quellgeister*, referring to the seven fundamental qualities or forces that flow through all existence.</note>), then the astringent quality was, in its engendering, at its most harsh and austere, and it strongly and violently constricted the operation of all the source-spirits. 16. When the love of God ignited the place of the sun, or the sun; then the terrible, harsh, and bitter explosion of fire (whose generation and original source are the bitter wrath of God, ignited in the <term>astringent quality<note>original: "qualité astringente"; the first of the seven source-spirits, representing the force of contraction, cold, and "hardness" that gives structure to matter.</note></term> through the water) first rose from the anguish, from the place of the sun, from the seven <term>source-spirits<note>original: "sources-esprits"; the seven fundamental properties or active forces of nature in Böhme’s mystical system.</note></term> of nature.

17. In the ignition of the sun, it first rose from the chamber of death; it awakened death; it began life, and ascended furious and trembling above itself, until the light of the sun seized it and impregnated it; then it was imprisoned by the sweetness of the light and remained there. It is from this that the planet Mars originated.

18. It is from this that the sensibility of nature, or the planet Jupiter, came.

30. 31. 55. Now, this is the *body* of the deep, or indeed the true chamber of death <note>In theosophical terms, "death" or "the chamber of death" often refers to the state of potentiality or stillness before the divine light or "mobility" animates it.</note>.

57. 59. Now, if the seven spirits do not struggle in a triumphant manner in some place, there is no mobility in that place, but a deep darkness. ### *Of man and the stars.*

71. Just as now the depth or the house of this world is a dark house—where corporality <note>The physical, material state of the universe.</note> (which is entirely opaque, obscure, full of anguish, and half-dead) is born and receives its movement from the planets and stars that ignite the body in the outermost generation <note>In this context, "generation" refers to the continuous process of "giving birth" to reality or the act of becoming.</note>, from which the movement of the elements results, as well as the shaped and created being—so too the flesh of man is a dark valley. In this valley, there is indeed an inner anguish for the birth of life, which tirelessly exerts itself to rise toward the light, from which life may be ignited.

72. But as the heart of God hides itself in the center or in the core, this cannot be <note>The author suggests that the divine spark is currently hidden within the material "husk" and cannot be fully reached by the physical body alone.</note>. Therefore, the anguish engenders nothing more than a seed. The house of flesh produces a seed similar to itself to form a man once more; and the house of spirit, according to the current state of the seven spirits, produces in the seed another spirit resembling itself to form a human spirit once more.

73. And the house of the hidden heart produces for him...

### CH. 26.

83. When this was done, the seven <term>source-spirits<note>Originally "sources-esprits" (German: *Quellgeister*). 84. Thus, man became a living soul, according to the manner and way in which the sun arose, followed by the seven planets.

85. The light that the heart of God breathed into man represents the sun that shines in the universal deep; this will be further clarified when I discuss the creation of man.

86. Now, observe. Just as from the *body of the dark deep*, through the igniting of the stars, a seed similar to the *creaturely body* <note>The physical, material form of a living being as opposed to its purely spiritual essence.</note> is generated within the depth of this world; in the same way and manner, a seed is generated within the house of flesh of man, according to the laws of the eternal generation of the seven source-spirits.

87. And within the seed there are three distinct things, among which one cannot scrutinize the other, and yet they exist within a single seed, and they *inqualify* together as though forming a single being. Thus they are but one being, yet three distinct things according to the form and manner of the Trinity within the Divinity.

88. First, there is the entire body of man, which is a dark house; outside of the *inqualification* of the seven-spirits, it has no mobility, but is a dark valley, similar to the body of the deep of this world.

### CH. 26.

89. Then, within the dark body of man, there exists the same governance—concerning the seven spirits—as exists within the body of the deep. 90. Now, this seed has a first mother, which is the <term>dark chamber<note>The physical material of the body before it is illuminated by spiritual light.</note></term> of the house of flesh. Thirdly, it has a mother that is generated in the circle of the seven spirits, in the center, and this is the heart of the seven spirits.

91. Such, then, is the mother of the soul, which shines through the seven spirits and makes them alive. In this same place, the seed <term>inqualifies</term> with the Heart of God—but only that seed in which the light is ignited. As for the seed in which the fire of wrath burns, the third mother remains imprisoned in the dark chamber.

92. And although she is the third mother, she nevertheless remains senseless if the light does not ignite within her; just as the depth of this world is inept in relation to the Heart of God, because the wheel of the seven spirits therein is surrendered to great anguish, to evil and good, to heat and cold, as is evident before our eyes.

93. But, when the third mother is ignited in the light, she is then in the created heaven of the holy life, and she fills the second mother with her radiance, from which the seven spirits receive a joyful will, which is the love of life, as <note>The text breaks off here at the end of the page.</note>

99. This is where the true foundation of man lies. Observe this precisely, for it is the mirror of a great mystery, the deep secret of humanity; this is why all the learned ones have circled around it since the beginning of the world, and have searched for this door, and yet have not found it.

100. But I must, once again, announce that it is the dawn of the day <note>original French: "l’aurore du jour", a likely reference to Boehme’s seminal work, *Aurora*</note>, just as the guardian of the gate has resolved.

104. 105. 106. But this glance was the spirit of love in the heart of God; this spirit impregnated, in the place of that glance, the oil of the water <note>a spiritual essence or "mercury" in Boehme's alchemy that serves as the medium for light</note>, from which the light had previously risen.

107. Think here of the glance given to St. Peter in the house of Caiaphas <note>refers to the Gospel account where Jesus looks at Peter after his denial, a glance of both conviction and divine mercy (Luke 22:61)</note>; it is exactly the same.

->*Now the question arises:*<-

Why then, at that moment, were there not many *masses* created, from which an entire legion of angels would have immediately and simultaneously emerged to take the place of the fallen Lucifer? Why was it necessary to languish for such a long time in wrath? And why must the entire legion be generated from a single *mass* over such a long period of time? Or rather, did the Creator not see and recognize the fall of man at that very moment?

<summary>This page explains how the primordial substance of man was protected by the Word to prevent the source-spirits from igniting prematurely, which might have led to a fall like Lucifer's. It concludes by raising philosophical questions about why humanity was created as a single lineage rather than a full legion of angels.</summary>
<keywords>salitter, source-spirits, mass, creation of man, Lucifer, divine love, Jacob Boehme, spiritual alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 362 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 26 of Jacob Boehme’s Aurora. The author is discussing the nature of divine revelation and the difficulty of articulating spiritual experiences through human language and reason.</meta>

->CH. 26<-

131. This is the true gate of the mystery of God, upon which the reader must notice that it would not be possible for a man to know and to understand such things if the **dawn** <note>original: "l’aurore" — a reference to the title of this work, symbolizing the beginning of divine enlightenment in the soul.</note> had not broken in the center of the soul. For these are divine secrets that no man could scrutinize by his own reason; therefore, I acknowledge myself to be most unworthy. I also expect that I shall not lack for mockers, for **corrupted nature** blushes terribly before the light.

132. But that will not make me let go. For if the divine light breaks into the circle of the generation of life, then the <term>source-spirits</term> <note>In Boehme’s philosophy, these are the fundamental qualities or "welling" forces—such as sweetness, bitterness, or heat—that constitute both the divine nature and the human soul.</note> rejoice, and see within the circle of life—in their mother, behind them in the divinity, and also before them in eternity.

133. However, this is not a permanent thing, nor a constant illumination of the source-spirits, and even less so of the animal body; rather, these are rays of the blossoming of God’s light through the **igneous impulse** <note>A "fire-like" drive or spiritual heat that triggers the movement toward divine love.</note>, which rises in love through the sweet water of life, and which remains in its heaven.

134. That is why I cannot carry this further than from the heart into the brain, before the **sovereign tribunal of thoughts**; there, it is enclosed in the firmament of heaven, and it does not return back through the source-spirits into the mother of the heart so that it might reach the tongue. If it were so, I could express it with my mouth and announce it to the world.

135. Therefore, I must leave it in

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his spiritual insights come from divine revelation rather than human reason, though he struggles to translate these celestial "flashes" into spoken language.</summary>
<keywords>divine mystery, source-spirits, Jacob Boehme, spiritual enlightenment, sovereign tribunal of thoughts, fire-impulse, Aurora</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Chapter 26 of Jacob Boehme’s Aurora, specifically continuing paragraph 134 where the author describes the struggle of translating divine revelation from the spiritual "firmament" into human thought and language. ->CH. 26.<-

his heaven, and write according to what is given to me, and consider with wonder what will result from it. For I cannot sufficiently grasp it in the <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "sources-esprits" (German: *Quellgeister*). In Boehme's cosmology, these are the seven active qualities—such as bitterness, sweetness, and heat—that "well up" to form all reality.</note>, since they are in the **chamber of anguish** <note>original: "chambre angoisseuse." This refers to the state of spiritual tension or "contraction" where the soul feels the lack of God before the light breaks through.</note>. I see it well according to the soul, but the firmament of heaven is in between. The soul hides in this firmament; it is there that it receives its rays from the light of God; this is why the light passes through this firmament of heaven, as if it were flashes of lightning, but in a completely gentle manner, spreading a delicious joy.

136. Thus, I cannot see this in the [*comprehensibility*] of my *current* source-spirits, or within the circle of life, unless the day breaks <note>A reference to the spiritual "dawn" or *Aurora* which gives the book its title.</note>. Therefore, I will set it forth according to the knowledge I have of it, even if the demon should ravage the world—which, however, he cannot do. But by this also, his hour or *his hourglass will be shown to him.*

137. Now come here, you partisans of **predestination** <note>Boehme is challenging the strict Calvinist doctrine that God has pre-selected certain individuals for salvation and others for damnation before birth.</note>, who believe yourselves to be on the right path, and who look upon **simple faith** as folly. You have circled for a long time around this point, and you have relied on the scriptures to say that God chose some men for the kingdom of heaven from their mother's womb, and that he rejected others.

138. If you can make for yourselves several *masses* <note>original: "masses." This refers to the biblical metaphor of the potter and the clay; Boehme argues that all humanity is made of the same spiritual "lump."</note> from which other men with other qualities could arise, then you would be right. But from a single *mass*, you can only make a single love of God, which penetrated the first man and penetrates...

<summary>Boehme describes the difficulty of articulating divine light through the "firmament" of human consciousness and begins a theological argument against the doctrine of predestination.</summary>

->CH. 26.<-

everywhere, and extends over all. Even if God had permitted Peter or Paul <note>The apostles Peter and Paul represent the highest scriptural authority; Boehme suggests that the internal "heart" of divine revelation is more fundamental than even the literal apostolic word.</note> to have written otherwise, you must nonetheless consider the **foundation**, or the **heart**. If only you reached the heart of God, your foundations would be sufficiently solid. If God lets me live a while longer, I will explain to you the **predestination** of Saint Paul <note>Referring to the Apostle Paul’s writings in the Book of Romans, which were the primary source for 17th-century debates on whether God chooses who is saved regardless of their actions.</note>.

## CHAPTER TWENTY-SEVEN.

### [ Author’s Note. ]

> ( “ **I WARN** the reader who loves God, that this
> ” book of **THE AURORA** <note>original: "L'AURORE" — This refers to Boehme's first work, *Morgenröte im Aufgang* (The Day-Spring), written in 1612.</note> was not completed, for the
> ” demon <note>Boehme refers here to the spiritual and earthly opposition he faced, which he viewed as a personified force of darkness.</note> proposed to place obstacles in its way, seeing
> ” that through it the day was about to appear. In effect, the day
> ” already pursues the **Aurora**, so that it is almost
> ” light. There should still be about thirty
> ” sheets <note>The author uses "feuilles" (leaves or sheets) to describe the remaining length of the manuscript.</note> to this work; but as the storm
> ” has made its eruption <note>The "storm" refers to the intense persecution Boehme faced from the local clergy in Görlitz, specifically Pastor Gregorius Richter, who had the manuscript seized and forbade Boehme from writing for several years.</note>, it was not finished. In
> ” the meantime, the day has come, so that the **Au-**
> ” **rora** has passed; and since that time the work
> ” has been done in the light of day. The work must therefore remain as
> ” it is, as an eternal memorial, especially
> ” since what is missing from it is restored in the other
> ” books.” )

->JACOB BOEHME.<-

Year 1620.

->END OF THE SECOND VOLUME.<-

<summary>This page features a concluding note by Jacob Boehme explaining that his seminal work, The Aurora, remained unfinished due to religious persecution, though he considers its message fulfilled in his later writings.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, The Aurora, religious persecution, spiritual enlightenment, manuscript history, The Day-Spring</keywords>
